# Issue #550 — minimal rustls advisory remediation

Worktree: `/Users/jirathip/.herdr/worktrees/corral/fix-rustls-advisory`.
Branch: `g549-rustls-advisory-fix`.
Pinned base: `d365a38116ddd90a79914f7f8a4ed4147aa69039`.
The preceding integration record is preserved byte-for-byte as this file's prefix.

## Change and resolver scope

Ran `cargo update -p rustls --precise 0.23.45`, raw exit **0**,
log `/tmp/g550-update.log`. Its version-update line was:

```text
    Updating rustls v0.23.43 -> v0.23.45
```

| Package | Base | Delivered | Other changes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| rustls | 0.23.43 | 0.23.45 | crates.io checksum for the patched release |

No transitive package version changes, additions, or removals. Parsing both
lockfiles with Python `tomllib` and comparing every package record yielded:

```text
package_deltas = [('rustls', '0.23.43', '0.23.45', ['checksum', 'version'])]
```

The resolver initially also changed tempfile 3.27.0's edge from the already-locked
getrandom 0.4.3 to the already-locked 0.3.4. That unrelated edge change was restored
to its base value before any GREEN gate. The installed tempfile manifest allows
`>=0.3.0, <0.5`; `cargo tree --locked -p rustls` and the locked deny gate both
accepted the restored edge. The delivered diff therefore contains only rustls's
version/checksum, not this incidental resolver churn. No Cargo.toml or policy
change was needed; `deny.toml` retains `ignore = []`.

`cargo tree --locked -p rustls` exited **0**, log `/tmp/g550-tree.log`:

```text
rustls v0.23.45
```

Filtered `git diff Cargo.lock` version lines captured before committing:

```diff
 name = "rustls"
-version = "0.23.43"
+version = "0.23.45"
```

```sh
cargo audit > /tmp/g550-audit.log 2>&1; echo AUDIT_EXIT=$?
```

## Blocker and attempted verification

The baseline native build failed with ENOSPC before any test executed. It ran
under the required shared native lock, in the named lane, before source edits:

```sh
flock /tmp/n.lock env HERDR_XCODEBUILD_DIRECT=1 HERMES_SIM_TASK_ACTIVE=1 \
  xcodebuild test -project ios/FleetNotifier.xcodeproj -scheme FleetNotifier \
  -destination "platform=iOS Simulator,id=59DDC0C5-891E-4EC0-91AF-4F50DF68D793" \
  -derivedDataPath /tmp/g552-base-dd \
  -only-testing:FleetNotifierTests/HerdRailZoneTests \
  > /tmp/g552-base-rail.log 2>&1; echo BASE_RAIL_EXIT=$?
```

Raw result: `BASE_RAIL_EXIT=65`. The shell wrapper exited 0 because its last
command printed that status; 65 is the actual xcodebuild verdict. The process
finished, without a tool timeout, within its first 95 seconds of observation.
This is a build/infrastructure failure, NOT regression-test RED evidence.

Selected raw output from `/tmp/g552-base-rail.log`:

```text
BOMStreamFlush: write: No space left on device
CoreUI: Error: unable to create storage file /tmp/g552-base-dd/Build/Intermediates.noindex/FleetNotifier.build/Debug-iphonesimulator/FleetNotifier.build/assetcatalog_output/thinned/Assets.car error 'No space left on device'

** TEST FAILED **
```

Xcode also reported that the result bundle could not be saved. The printed
xcresult path is therefore NOT asserted to be usable evidence; the raw text
log is retained. Immediately after the failure, `df -h / /tmp` reported
964 MiB available, and `du -sh /tmp/g552-base-dd /tmp/g552-scratch` reported
70 MiB and 96 MiB respectively. A later saved `df` receipt reported 1.9 GiB
available and the Data volume at 100% capacity. Available space was changing
outside this lane; no stable build/capture capacity is established.

No other lane's files, caches, processes, or simulators were reclaimed. The
unused disposable worktree created by this lane at `/tmp/g552-scratch` was
removed after verifying its base and clean status. The failed DerivedData and
logs remain. No system changes, installs, source workarounds, or repeated
native build attempts were made. Owner/orchestrator must provide sufficient

# Issue #552 — repository chip hierarchy + shared HUD rhythm (implementation report)

Lane: implementer · Worktree: `/Users/jirathip/.herdr/worktrees/corral/impl552-herd-chip`
Branch: `g552-herd-chip-sizing` · Exact source base: `b6b69f377883d6b2507f8418b6b4dc34f7452145`
(`integration`, includes #547/#548/#550).

This section SUPERSEDES the round-1 "BLOCKED before implementation" receipt above, which is
preserved byte-for-byte as this file's earlier section. The implementation commit is `a5ec480`
("fix(ios): anchor Herd repository chip in the HUD rhythm"); the orchestrator's WIP-preservation
commit `22e6986` carries this lane's round-2 evidence artifacts. This round adopted that state
(no revert, no history rewrite), re-ran every gate at the delivered source bytes, and finished the
measured evidence. **No iOS source byte changed in this round**: `HerdView.swift` SHA-256
`a533d3d9…` and `HerdTests.swift` SHA-256 `c6bb8bdf…` are the bytes the gates below ran against and
the bytes this branch delivers (`docs/evidence/issue-552/SHA256SUMS`, `shasum -a 256 -c` → 16/16 OK,
exit 0).

## 1. Type hierarchy in the chip (required work 1)

`ios/FleetNotifier/UI/Herd/HerdView.swift:365-399` — `repositoryChip`:

| file:line | delivered code |
| --- | --- |
| `:369` | `let name = Text(paddock.title).font(.subheadline.weight(.semibold))` |
| `:370` | `.foregroundColor(ranchTokens.inkColor)` |
| `:371` | `let counts = Text(herdRepositoryCaption(paddock).dropFirst(paddock.title.count))` |
| `:372` | `.font(.caption).foregroundColor(ranchTokens.mutedColor)` |
| `:380-381` | `Text("\(name)\(counts)").fixedSize(horizontal:false,vertical:true)` |
| `:386-387` | `.padding(.horizontal,12).padding(.vertical,6)` / `.ranchChromeSurface(ranchTokens)` |
| `:388` | `.accessibilityLabel(herdRepositoryCaption(paddock))` |

The name is primary (`ranchTokens.inkColor`, `.subheadline.weight(.semibold)` — Apple's default
15 pt at Large); the `· N here` / `· N at rail` half stays `.caption` (12 pt) in the muted ink, so
the count is secondary. The caption string itself is unchanged: `herdRepositoryCaption` at `:525-528`
keeps its content rules (the `at rail` suffix only when `blockedCount > 0`), still pinned by
`HerdTests.swift:418` (`testRepositoryCaptionOnlyAddsPositiveRailCounts`). Day/Night uses only the
existing `ranchTokens` (`inkColor`, `mutedColor`) — no new colors, no new tokens, no art change.

## 2. Chip form and chrome (required work 2)

Still a chip, not a bar: the surface keeps intrinsic width and is only left-aligned
(`HerdView.swift:394` `.frame(maxWidth:.infinity,alignment:.leading)`), and it now carries exactly
the counters strip's chrome call and vertical padding:

| | counters strip (`statusSummary`, `:298-311`) | repository chip (`:365-399`) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| padding | `.padding(.vertical,6).padding(.horizontal,12)` (`:306`) | `.padding(.horizontal,12).padding(.vertical,6)` (`:386`) |
| surface | `.ranchChromeSurface(ranchTokens)` (`:308`) | `.ranchChromeSurface(ranchTokens)` (`:387`) |
| corner radius | default (15 pt, `UI/FleetViews.swift:3267-3268`) | same default |

The chip no longer passes the old bespoke `cornerRadius: 8`; only the agent cards still do
(`:485`), which is what the wiring test now asserts (`HerdTests.swift:1971-1972`, "agent cards
retain compact corners; #552 chip uses the counters strip's default chrome").

## 3. OWNER AMENDMENT — the two HUD gaps are equal (required work 3)

**Constant reused** — `ios/FleetNotifier/UI/Herd/HerdView.swift:40`, quoted verbatim:

```swift
    private let hudSpacing: CGFloat = 6
```

It is the HUD stack's own spacing: `HerdView.swift:227` is `VStack(spacing:hudSpacing) {` (the
Filters/connection row → counters strip gap; the pre-change code spelled the same value inline as
`spacing:6`). The chip container reuses that same constant for its top gap at `:395`:
`.padding(.horizontal,12).padding(.top,hudSpacing)`. No new spacing value was introduced — the only
value in play is the pre-existing `6`.

**Measured, both gaps, Day and Night** (G1, `HerdRailZoneTests` /
`testRepositoryChipSharesHUDRhythmAndKeepsIntrinsicChrome`, `G552_MEASURE` lines in
`/tmp/g552-focused.log`; machine-readable copy in `docs/evidence/issue-552/layout-measurements.json`):

| Dynamic Type | Filters→counters | counters→chip | equal |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| `.large` | 6.0 pt | 6.0 pt | yes (Δ = 0.0) |
| `.accessibility3` | 6.0 pt | 6.0 pt | yes (Δ = 0.0) |
| `.accessibility5` | 6.0 pt | 6.0 pt | yes (Δ = 0.0) |

Night values are identical to Day for all three sizes. Raw uncompressed line (Day, `.large`):

```text
G552_MEASURE night=false type=large filtersToCounts=5.999999999999986 countsToChip=6.0 chipWidth=172.66666666666666 countsWidth=366.0 textHeight=21.0 countsTextHeight=16.33333333333333
```

`filtersToCounts` is computed from the rendered frames as `hud-counts.minY - hud-row.maxY`, and
`countsToChip` as `repository-surface.minY - hud-counts.maxY` (frames from the production views, not
a parallel layout model). The test asserts `XCTAssertEqual(firstGap, 6, accuracy: 0.01)` and
`XCTAssertEqual(secondGap, firstGap, accuracy: 0.01)` (`HerdTests.swift:385-386`).

**Before vs after, same frames** (`docs/evidence/issue-552/layout-frames.json`, base `b6b69f3` run
vs delivered run):

| measurement (Day, `.large`, state `empty`) | base `b6b69f3` | delivered head |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Filters→counters gap | 6.0 pt (source-derived: the base source spells the same VStack literal `spacing:6`; the base run predates the `hud-row`/`hud-counts` probes, which is why that one cell is not frame-measured) | 6.0 pt (frame-measured) |
| counters→chip-surface gap | **2.0 pt** (frame-measured: chip container `y=84.33` + its base `.padding(.top,2)`) | **6.0 pt** (frame-measured) |
| chip container (y / height) | 84.33 / 22.33 | 84.33 / 39.0 |
| chip surface height | 20.33 pt | 33.0 pt |

The counters strip's bottom edge is 84.33 pt in both trees (base: measured as the chip container's
start, since the two share a `spacing:0` stack; head: measured directly as `hud-counts.maxY`), so the
two rows above are the same measurement basis. The owner's complaint was exactly this asymmetry
(6 vs 2 pt); the delivered HUD rhythm is 6 vs 6.

## 4. Chip metrics — measured, not eyeballed

| metric (blocked scope; Day and Night identical) | chip | counters strip |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| width, `.large` | 172.67 pt | 366.0 pt |
| width, `.accessibility3` | 293.67 pt | 366.0 pt |
| width, `.accessibility5` | 355.33 pt | 366.0 pt |
| width, `.large`, empty scope | 122.0 pt | 366.0 pt |
| height, `.large` | 33.0 pt = 6 + 21.0 + 6 | 28.33 pt = 6 + 16.33 + 6 |
| text line height, `.large` | 21.0 pt (name path) | 16.33 pt |
| corner radius | `ranchChromeSurface` default (15 pt) | same default |

The chip is **narrower than the counters strip in every measured configuration** (172.67/293.67/
355.33/122.0 vs 366.0 — i.e. 33%-47% of the strip), so it is still a chip and not a full-width bar.
The name's line box (21.0 pt) is one type step above the counters text (16.33 pt, +4.67 pt), which
is the declared role step `.subheadline.weight(.semibold)` (15 pt) vs `.caption2` monospaced (11 pt).

## 5. Accessibility sizes (required work 5)

At `.accessibility3`/`.accessibility5` the chip's name+count text wraps instead of truncating:
measured text height 95.0 pt (a3) and 128.0 pt (a5), rendered inside chip surfaces of 111.0 pt and
146.67 pt respectively (the surface is a few points taller than its text because the chip keeps a
two-line `.subheadline` reserve so a rail occupancy change cannot reflow the row —
`HerdView.swift:374-379`). The chip carries no `.lineLimit` at all (`HerdTests.swift:414` asserts its
absence), and the same test proves the text frame stays inside the chrome with the full 12 pt
horizontal and 6 pt vertical padding at every size (`HerdTests.swift:389-392`) — nothing is clipped.
Screenshots:
`docs/evidence/issue-552/accessibility3-day.png`, `accessibility5-day.png` (the test's own 1×
`XCTAttachment` captures for the a3/a5 runs, exported with `xcresulttool`; receipt
`/tmp/g552-export-attachments.log`, manifest `/tmp/g552-evidence/accessibility/manifest.json`).

## 8. Gates — exact commands, raw exit codes, logs

All commands ran from this worktree. Raw exit status is the verdict; no `| grep`/`| tail` is used as
a PASS/FAIL signal, and log parsing happened only after each raw exit was captured. Simulator/build
legs were serialized host-wide under `flock /tmp/n.lock` exactly as briefed (the G2 leg queued ~30
minutes behind another lane's measurement leg before acquiring the lock).

```sh

flock /tmp/n.lock env HERDR_XCODEBUILD_DIRECT=1 HERMES_SIM_TASK_ACTIVE=1 \
  xcodebuild test -project ios/FleetNotifier.xcodeproj -scheme FleetNotifier \
  -destination "platform=iOS Simulator,id=59DDC0C5-891E-4EC0-91AF-4F50DF68D793" \
  -derivedDataPath /tmp/g552-dd \

  -only-testing:FleetNotifierTests/FullScreenHerdShellWiringTests \
  -only-testing:FleetNotifierTests/FullScreenHerdShellAccessibilityLayoutTests \
  > /tmp/g552-focused.log 2>&1; echo FOCUSED_EXIT=$?

# G2 full suite
flock /tmp/n.lock env HERDR_XCODEBUILD_DIRECT=1 HERMES_SIM_TASK_ACTIVE=1 \
  xcodebuild test -project ios/FleetNotifier.xcodeproj -scheme FleetNotifier \
  -destination "platform=iOS Simulator,id=59DDC0C5-891E-4EC0-91AF-4F50DF68D793" \
  -derivedDataPath /tmp/g552-dd -only-testing:FleetNotifierTests \
  > /tmp/g552-full.log 2>&1; echo FULL_EXIT=$?

# G3 boundary + self-test
python3 ios/check-release-demo.py > /tmp/g552-check-release.log 2>&1; echo CHECK_RELEASE_EXIT=$?
python3 ios/check-release-demo.py --self-test > /tmp/g552-self-test.log 2>&1; echo SELF_TEST_EXIT=$?

# G4 Debug + Release builds + Release binary check (each leg under the same lock)
flock /tmp/n.lock env HERDR_XCODEBUILD_DIRECT=1 HERMES_SIM_TASK_ACTIVE=1 \
  xcodebuild build -project ios/FleetNotifier.xcodeproj -scheme FleetNotifier -configuration Debug \
  -destination 'generic/platform=iOS Simulator' -derivedDataPath /tmp/g552-debug-dd \

flock /tmp/n.lock env HERDR_XCODEBUILD_DIRECT=1 HERMES_SIM_TASK_ACTIVE=1 \
  xcodebuild build -project ios/FleetNotifier.xcodeproj -scheme FleetNotifier -configuration Release \

python3 ios/check-release-demo.py --binary \
  /tmp/g552-release-dd/Build/Products/Release-iphonesimulator/FleetNotifier.app/FleetNotifier \
  > /tmp/g552-binary.log 2>&1; echo BINARY_EXIT=$?

# G5 project-generation drift (expect empty diff)
(cd ios && xcodegen generate --spec project.yml) > /tmp/g552-xcodegen.log 2>&1; \
  echo XCODEGEN_EXIT=$?; git diff --exit-code -- ios/ > /tmp/g552-xcodegen-diff.log 2>&1; \
  echo XCODEGEN_DIFF_EXIT=$?

# G6 advisory anti-slop (identity-compare base vs head; zero ADDED)
swift run --package-path ios/tools/anti-slop-swift anti-slop \
  ios/FleetNotifier ios/FleetNotifierTests > /tmp/g552-aslop-head.log 2>&1; echo ASLOP_EXIT=$?

# G7 hygiene

# G8 mutation battery
bash /tmp/g552-probes.sh > /tmp/g552-probes.log 2>&1; echo PROBES_EXIT=$?
```

# Integration merges — resumed mission window (2026-09-15, later)

Under the owner's resumed mandate ("adopt the preserved partial branches, apply the #552 spacing
amendment, gates + independent review at the exact head, merge to integration on PASS"), two lanes were
resumed from their preserved WIP states and merged here after independent adversarial review at their
exact delivered heads.

## #552 — Herd repository chip sized as the paddock title (HUD rhythm gap equality)

- Branch `g552-herd-chip-sizing`, reviewed head `5998a5538cafe480b42541c30c5dd33c345c8c12`.
- Review: PASS (`rev552-chip-r1`, fleet-rev, fresh read-only worktree). Its own `hudSpacing` 6→10 probe
  went assertion-RED at exit 65 (`XCTAssertEqualWithAccuracy "9.999999999999986" != "6.0" ± 0.01`),
  proving one shared constant drives both HUD gaps, and restored byte-identically.
- Owner amendment verified: `hudSpacing = 6` (`HerdView.swift:40`) reused at `:227` (Filters→counters)
  and `:395` (counters→chip); measured gaps equal (6.0pt) at `.large`, `.accessibility3`,
  `.accessibility5`, Day and Night; chip stays narrower than the counters strip (172.67/293.67 vs
  366.0pt) with a taller name (21.0 vs 16.33pt text height).
- Merge `b4515cc27898bc51461de3aad46b117d03e2e05b` (tree identical to the reviewed head).
- Merged-head gates (orch runs): `FleetNotifierTests` 632/0 exit 0, `check-release-demo.py` 0,
  xcodegen drift 0 with an empty `ios/` diff, `git diff --check` 0; hosted CI on that head: Secret scan,
  iOS art/icon gates and Linux installer gate all success.
- Flagged, not silently accepted: the absolute paddock origin moved 290.33 → 307.0pt (+16.67) because
  the chip is taller. The #548 occupancy-invariance property still holds and the shift is disclosed;
  the owner was asked whether he meant the strict "origin does not move" reading (the issue stays open
  on his device gate either way).

## #551 — warm return keeps last-known Herd state and withholds motion until the first verified frame

- Branch `g551-warm-return-reconnect`, reviewed head `0b7f085272ada92c9b3929c578974d23ded6682c`.
- Three independent adversarial reviews in fresh read-only worktrees:
  - `18077661dcfadac03af44a21e433876f1c3b6549` → **FAIL**: the measurement was sound, but the
    "no client-side stage is implicated / no production fix" decision was mis-shaped —
    `UI/Herd/HerdModel.swift:55` recast every retained row to `.unknown` for the whole warm-return
    window, which renders the owner's reported `N unknown` counters strip (executed probe, exit 65).
  - `ae9a885ad706896d2aea845795a64f54bc8203a2` → **FAIL**: the fix was correct, but removing the recast
    un-suppressed the clock-driven `y:` bob on retained rows (`HerdView.swift:500-501`, executed probe:
    17 distinct offsets across an 8s sweep on a retained idle row) and the round-2 ledger adjustment at
    `HerdTests.swift:78-80` had dropped the only retained-row `reduceMotion: false` assertion, leaving
    the new `HerdModel.swift:78` graze guard unpinned (removing it kept all 637 tests green).
  - `0b7f085` → **PASS** (final round): the reviewer executed the bob fence itself (retained idle row
    constant across the sweep, verified idle row still animating), red-probed every new pin in a
    disposable tree with byte-identical restores, confirmed the assertion ledger is clean (the three
    deletions over `b4515cc..HEAD` are the round-2 recast inversions, each replaced by strictly stronger
    last-known assertions), confirmed the §8.1/§8.1a/§8.3/§8.4 corrections are real (its independently
    recomputed numbers match the report), and confirmed no device/CI/PR claim anywhere.
- Merge `e5328c5` (tree identical to the reviewed head).
- Delivered content: `HerdModel.state` is the last-known token (recast removed); `bob`, `roam`, `gait`
  and the idle grazing pose are all guarded by `!disconnected`, so a retained row is drawn statically
  until the first verified frame; Herd-surface guards + their pins; the measured stage/rendered evidence
  and the device protocol; the client-side timing chain measured with no regression, so no timing, SSE,
  trust or daemon code is touched.
- Merged-head gates (orch runs): `FleetNotifierTests` 639 tests / 1 skipped / 0 failures exit 0,
  `check-release-demo.py` exit 0, `git diff --check` exit 0; digest pins re-pinned over the round-3
  source sets (fail-closed, non-weakening).
- Lane report preserved in-tree at `docs/evidence/issue-551/lane-report.md` (this file's content on the
  lane branch, which replaced the integration record during the rebase).

load 462/10 cores, and `/snapshot` took 0.96 s / 2.45 s / 8.36 s (0.001 s idle). Both installers pinned the

## 2. Contract conflict I had to resolve (ProcessType scope) — read this before reviewing

The brief's contract #1 and AC #1 say the corrald plist must use `Interactive` "in **ALL THREE** plist
emitters (`:155`, `:230`, `:274`)" / "**The three emitters** and the Linux `[Service]` carry the new values".
Sites `:230`/`:274` are the **update-checker** and **log-rotation** agents, so this conflicts with contract
#1's next sentence ("The update-checker plist **MAY** stay `Background`") and with AC #4 ("assert … that the
update-checker plist is unaffected"), and it is wider than the issue body's own wording ("Change the corrald
plist to Interactive (the update-checker plist may stay Background)").

**Delivered: the enumerated reading** — all three emitted agents are `Interactive` — because that is what the
brief's contract #1 and AC #1 say and "MAY stay Background" is permissive, not requiring. AC #4 is then
satisfied as far as it can be truthfully: the tests assert the update/rotate plists are unaffected **beyond
the ProcessType line** (byte-diff is ProcessType-only and exactly one key each), and that no migration path
touches them in any other way. The report does **not** claim their tier stayed `Background`.

If the owner wants the narrower issue reading, it is two one-line reversals: `scripts/setup-corrald.sh:253`
and `:298` back to `Background`, and drop the matching report calls at `:266`/`:311` (+ their assertions).
The corrald tier (`:177`) and every Linux change are independent of that choice.

Both suites end with their own PASS line: `OK: daemon launchd plist (shared PATH, ~/.local/bin, gh
resolution, XML escaping, no token, idempotence, #555 Interactive tier + migration)` and `OK: install-corral
Linux/systemd scenarios passed (24 logs …)`. The Linux suite now reports ten scenarios, including `ok:
pre-#555 migration: reported, scheduling-only diff, byte-identical to fresh`. The macOS suite is
macOS-only by design (it exits `SKIP` elsewhere) and is not wired into a hosted workflow.

Portability trap found and fixed in this lane: macOS `/bin/bash` is **3.2.57**, which cannot parse a heredoc
inside a command substitution at all (`x="$(cat <<'EOF' … EOF)"` → `unexpected EOF while looking for matching
`''`). The new expected-text blocks therefore use `IFS= read -r -d '' var <<'EOF' || true` with the value held
in a variable. `bash -n` over `scripts/*.sh` is the gate that catches this on this host.

| `p2-corrald-process-type` | only the corrald agent → `Background` | RED | **1** | same assertion (the daemon's own tier is what the test owns) | byte-identical |
| `p3-duplicate-process-type` | second ProcessType key in the corrald emitter (what an append-style migration leaves) | RED | **1** | `FAIL: com.corral.corrald.plist has 2 ProcessType keys (want 1)` | byte-identical |
| `p4-append-instead-of-replace` | `cat > "$PLIST"` → `cat >> "$PLIST"` (non-idempotent migration) | RED | **1** | `FAIL: rerunning setup changed the daemon plist` | byte-identical |
| `p5-drop-nice-cpuweight` | remove `Nice=`/`CPUWeight=` from `render_unit` | RED | **1** | `FAIL: unit file … missing: 'Nice=-5'` | byte-identical |

No probe log contains a shell/syntax error line (checked: `syntax-error-lines: 0` for all six), i.e. every RED

## 5. Migration and idempotence

- **macOS** (`scripts/test-daemon-launchd-env.sh:175-211`): the three plists are rewritten to their pre-#555
  bytes (`ProcessType=Background`; `sed` line substitution, so nothing else differs), the installer is
  re-run, and the suite asserts: `ProcessType` == `Interactive` for all three agents; exactly one
  `ProcessType` key each; the pre-#555↔migrated byte diff is **exactly two lines, both ProcessType**; each
  migrated plist `cmp`-equals the fresh-install snapshot taken at the start of the run; the three
  `migrated … ProcessType: Background -> Interactive` lines are printed; and a further run leaves the bytes
  unchanged.
- **Linux** (`scripts/test-install-corral-linux.sh:253-349`): a pre-#555 unit (the emitter's exact pre-#555
  text, in its own `$HOME`/install root) is present before the install; the suite asserts the report line,
  `Nice=-5`/`CPUWeight=500`, no `CPUSchedulingPolicy=`, the `[Service]` section verbatim, **no removed or
  replaced pre-#555 line**, the added lines to be exactly the four #555 lines, and the migrated unit
  (home-normalized) to be byte-identical to the fresh-install unit.
- Fresh == migrated also holds by construction: the plists are rewritten whole from one heredoc and the unit
  is rendered whole into a temp file and `mv`-ed in, so a re-run can neither accumulate keys nor grow —
  which is exactly what `p3`/`p4` prove the tests catch if that ever stops being true.

# #555 daemon worker — blocked at scope preflight

Status: BLOCKED; no implementation or performance claim.

BLOCKER: tests/http.rs:171-195 — the mandatory workspace gate asserts that a `/snapshot` request immediately flushes a just-applied change, without awaiting publication. The requested debounced, pre-serialized read path must instead serve the last published buffer without flushing, serializing, or walking the agent map. The minimal correct change includes updating this HTTP test to await explicit publication, but `tests/**` is outside the brief's allowed file fence. Per `.brief.md:61`, stopped before implementation/heavy execution.

Minimal scope decision requested: allow targeted publication-boundary adjustments in `tests/http.rs`, while retaining all schema, field-value, epoch, cursor and SSE assertions. This is not permission to weaken latency, byte-equality or epoch coverage. New cache/regression tests can remain inside `src/**` as required.

## Verified checkout and contract

- Worktree: `/Users/jirathip/.herdr/worktrees/corral/impl555-daemon`.
- Branch: `g555-daemon-snapshot`.
- Source/base head inspected: `97ed9e03cb4d265da02ac28bad1685000b159f80`. It had not advanced when rechecked. No rebase, merge, checkout or other-branch modification was performed.
- Read `.brief.md` and `.hermes-context.md` completely, plus issue #555 body and comments, including ROUTED and the sequenced daemon-worker plan.
- No AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md or justfile was found by root/target-directory discovery and tracked guidance-file lookup. No canonical just recipe was invented.

## Conflict evidence

1. `tests/http.rs:37-45` starts the background coalescer and constructs the router before the test writes state.
2. `tests/http.rs:171-180` awaits `store.apply(...)` and immediately executes `/snapshot`; there is no intervening `flush()` or publication wait.
3. `tests/http.rs:193-195` requires revision 1 and the newly applied blocked agent in that immediate response.
4. `src/api/mod.rs:187-190` currently satisfies this by awaiting `store.snapshot()` and serializing on the request path.
5. `src/core/store.rs:345-356` flushes and clones the agent map in `snapshot()`. The background coalescer otherwise waits the existing two-second debounce (`src/core/store.rs:250-269`). Merely switching the handler to the published cache makes the HTTP test's immediate-response expectation incompatible with that boundary.
6. A second boundary assertion, `tests/http.rs:223-229`, changes the shared backlog atomic and immediately expects new snapshot bytes without a publication wait. Backlog refresh must be covered explicitly rather than silently freezing that field in a cache.

Rejected workarounds: a test-only uncached handler would not verify production; retaining a request-time flush/serialization defeats the issue; publishing and bumping revision on every individual apply defeats batching (`tests/store.rs:29-43` expects three sequential applies to produce one revision). No such workaround was implemented.

This is a source-contract preflight finding, NOT an observed failure of an implemented cache. There is no cache diff and no RED/GREEN runtime claim.

# #555 daemon worker — resumed under the test-scope amendment

Status: daemon acceptance criteria verified. The earlier BLOCKED section is historical; the amendment resolved that scope conflict. Its bytes are retained, not silently rewritten. This is not a claim that the separate iOS confirmation or owner warm-return gate has passed.

Production/test source head: `2d09b37762b97a751a0eb52c5c0ac57ece1d5485`.
Baseline source head: `11cc1d26f3b1c22716d4527fb9e7679eaa861aba`.
The baseline-only step was committed as `918e778` before the implementation. The delivery commit adds evidence/report and evidence-driver corrections only; its source/test bytes are identical to the gated source head. The final response supplies the delivery and verified remote SHA.

## Changes and boundaries

- `src/core/store/published.rs:12`: immutable publication with `Arc<Bytes>` JSON and initial SSE frame, revision, capture time and bounded pre-encoded delta history. JSON serialization and frame construction happen at publication, not on HTTP reads.
- `src/core/store.rs:38-43,62-70,279-376,515-520`: startup publication, pointer-only reads, watch publication signal, serialized capture/encoding/publication, and a debounced publisher. The owned publication-lock guard travels into the blocking encoder, so cancellation cannot release publication ordering while an encoder remains in flight. The pointer is replaced before notifying live delta subscribers.
- `src/core/store.rs:291`: a foreground subscriber shortens an already-pending background deadline; repeated writes cannot extend it indefinitely. An idle tick republishes an independently changing git-plane backlog flag without inventing a delta/revision.
- `src/api/mod.rs:184`: `/snapshot` returns the published bytes, without flushing, JSON encoding, or walking the agent map. Info tracing records `serve_ms`, monotonic `buffer_age_ms` and revision.
- `src/api/events.rs:19`: subscribe before capturing the publication; use pre-encoded initial/replay bytes, preserve epoch/cursor rules and keepalive framing, and log first-frame timing plus publication age. Lag recovery subscribes afresh before taking its replacement snapshot, avoiding repeated lag snapshots from unread old deltas.
- `src/main.rs:411-432`: a dedicated `corral-http` OS thread/current-thread Tokio runtime owns the transferred listener. Background planes and the publisher cannot occupy its executor queue. `/host-key` uses that same HTTP executor without needing the store publication lock.
- `src/core/store/publication_tests.rs:1-369`, `tests/http.rs` and `tests/snapshot_load.rs`: independent wire-byte oracles, writer/publisher-lock barriers, publication and SSE races, bounded HTTP publication, and the unchanged before/after load harness.
- `docs/evidence/issue-555/daemon-run.py`: bounded commands under `/tmp/n.lock`, explicit `/tmp/g555-daemon-target`, disk/peer preflight, raw receipts, and owned nice-0 hog cleanup. `daemon-smoke.py` exercises the built daemon with scratch configuration and poisoned PATH. `daemon-mutations.py` executes selected discriminating mutations and restores source bytes.

No edits to `crates/**`, `ios/**`, `scripts/**`, `.github/**`, Cargo manifests/lockfile, `deny.toml`, or `fastlane/**`. No dependencies added. Compiling the existing workspace client crate is not an edit to it.

Structural inspection receipts: `ast-grep outline src/core/store/published.rs src/core/store.rs src/api/events.rs src/api/mod.rs src/main.rs --view signatures --match 'PublishedSnapshot|flush|run_coalescer|snapshot|events|published|main'` exited 0 and located the publication type, snapshot/events handlers and daemon entry points (`g555-final-structure.log.gz`). `ast-grep outline src/core/store.rs --view expanded --match Store` exited 0 and located `published` at 279, `publications` at 285, `run_coalescer` at 291, `flush` at 316 and `subscribe` at 515 (`g555-store-structure.log.gz`).

## Every changed test-file range and why

Ranges below are the added-line ranges from `git diff --unified=0 11cc1d26f3b1c22716d4527fb9e7679eaa861aba -- tests/http.rs tests/snapshot_load.rs src/core/store/publication_tests.rs`:

| File and current lines | Reason |
| --- | --- |
| `tests/http.rs:48-61` | Publication-watch await, explicit 3-second timeout and elapsed-time assertion. |
| `tests/http.rs:187` | Subscribe to publication before applying the named test's change. |
| `tests/http.rs:192-195` | Prove a request does not flush; await publication before all original visibility/schema assertions. |
| `tests/http.rs:244` | Subscribe before the independent backlog flag changes. |
| `tests/http.rs:248` | Await publication before the original backlog visibility assertion. |
| `tests/http.rs:251-260` | Also prove the reverse flag transition publishes, without a fabricated revision. |
| `tests/snapshot_load.rs:1-131` | New measurement-only benchmark, frozen and executed at baseline and after the implementation. |
| `src/core/store/publication_tests.rs:1-369` | New five-test publication/byte-contract/race suite. |

No existing HTTP assertion was removed or weakened; its diff contains additions only. No existing test was skipped or made conditional. The new load benchmark is explicitly ignored in ordinary workspace runs to keep the stress measurement opt-in, and was actually executed with `--ignored` for both measured legs. The ordinary workspace's ignored count is reported below, not concealed.

Debounce bound: `FOREGROUND_TICK = 250ms`; `BACKGROUND_TICK = 2s`, never extended by later writes. The comment next to those constants explicitly gives the tested publication deadline of 3s: the debounce plus 1s encoding/scheduling allowance. This is a testable deadline, not a hard real-time OS guarantee. Removing the publication swap causes the updated HTTP test to fail with `snapshot must publish within 3s: Elapsed(())` rather than waiting forever.

## Exact gates and real results

No justfile exists in this checkout. Commands ran from this worktree, using the brief's exact cargo entries. For example: `gzip -dc docs/evidence/issue-555/g555-workspace-test.log.gz`.

## Byte equality and read isolation

The randomized test's real output:

    G555_BYTE_EQUALITY states=64 snapshots=64 sse_frames=64 empty=1 legacy_header_cases=16 epochs=64 revisions=64

It publishes through the real Store and exercises the real router. Agent maps/revisions are seeded-randomized; each Store supplies a fresh OS-random lifetime epoch. Fresh reference serialization uses independently retained prior axum `Json`/`Sse(Event::json_data)` encoders, the actual agent state, and the same state-capture timestamp. It does not deserialize the cached agent map as its oracle. Both the complete JSON body and complete initial SSE frame are compared byte-for-byte. Empty startup, legacy absent-epoch headers, unknown epochs, future cursors and escaped/Unicode content are included.

Additional passing tests hold both the writer and publication locks while serving `/snapshot`, `/host-key` and initial/resume `/events` responses under a 50ms deadline; retain an old buffer across a later publication; compare cached resume with the existing epoch/history rules; and test snapshot-subscribe races plus recovery after overflowing the broadcast ring. Existing HTTP tests still verify lower/equal/higher epoch restarts, fresh/stale cursors, snapshot-then-live ordering and no fabricated deltas.

Discrimination probes (each compiled and failed at its assertion, not merely at compilation):

| Mutation | Exact focused test/command | Raw exit and assertion |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Put request-time `store.snapshot().await` back in `/snapshot`. | `cargo test --release --lib core::store::publication_tests::reads_and_initial_sse_do_not_wait_for_writer_or_publisher -- --exact --nocapture` | 101: `read queued behind writer/publisher: Elapsed(())`. |
| Put the same blocking read in `/events`. | Same exact command as above. | 101: same lock-barrier assertion. |
| Encode the wrong wire epoch. | `cargo test --release --lib core::store::publication_tests::randomized_publications_match_old_snapshot_and_sse_bytes -- --exact --nocapture` | 101: `assertion left == right failed: snapshot case 0`. |
| Drop the publication swap. | `cargo test --release --test http snapshot_returns_json_with_rev_and_agents -- --exact --nocapture` | 101: `snapshot must publish within 3s: Elapsed(())`. |

All source/test bytes were restored to the gated head; `git diff --exit-code HEAD -- src tests` returned 0. The two restored controls in the gate table ran after the final mutation. The full workspace gate ran once on those identical source bytes before the mutation probes; it was not relabeled as a post-probe full-suite run.

## Loaded performance: baseline first, identical harness

The finalized measurement harness SHA-256 is `08774414aa7681db131a8c4a18f86131832785adab4ad9a2f77384a8e3ebe27b`; it is unchanged between baseline and after. `baseline-source.json` records an empty production diff at `11cc1d26f3b1c22716d4527fb9e7679eaa861aba`. An initial measurement preceded harness formatting; both initial logs are retained, but the authoritative comparison uses the finalized harness's `baseline-final` run, still before any production edits.

Both legs use:

    cargo test --release --test snapshot_load -- --ignored --nocapture

Both commands exited 0. The runner's after-load budget check also passed. Build preparation was `cargo test --release --test snapshot_load --no-run`, exit 0, before starting the hogs in each measured leg.

The fixture has 64 agents, four Tokio runtime workers, and a continuously active synthetic poller performing 25ms of work while holding the same store lock used by integration/herdr updates. Ten owned `/usr/bin/yes` processes at nice level 0 saturated this ten-core host; the recorded top samples showed 0% idle after the change (and effectively saturated baseline samples). This is a synthetic busy-poller experiment, not a live git/GitHub/herdr fleet replay.

The measurement is daemon-side router middleware: entry through production routing to fully encoded response-body collection, including handler instrumentation. JSON inspection and sample logging happen after the timed interval. It is not curl/TCP/TLS latency. Quantiles are nearest-rank over 512 samples per leg.

| Metric | Baseline (`baseline-final`) | After (`after-load`) |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| `/snapshot` p50, ms | 43.870792 | 0.022917 |
| `/snapshot` p99, ms | 45.687750 | 0.065291 |
| Buffer age p50, ms | 1 | 873 |
| Buffer age p99, ms | 1 | 1980 |
| Buffer age max, ms | 2 | 2011 |
| Response bytes, min/max | 29129 / 29323 | 29129 / 29193 |

Baseline exceeded the 20ms p50 budget; its p99 was already below 50ms in this synthetic fixture. After satisfies both strict budgets. Revisions 1 and 2 were observed after the change, proving the publisher made progress during the measurement rather than serving one frozen buffer. The higher after-buffer age is the intentional bounded publication tradeoff; HTTP reads no longer force freshness by waiting behind the poller. Benchmark age is derived from `generated_at`; production info logs use monotonic time since capture.

Reproduce the before leg at the baseline-only commit and the after leg at the source head, respectively, with the committed runner; each fresh target should be built before `--hog`. Example after invocation:

    python3 docs/evidence/issue-555/daemon-run.py --hog --timeout 120 after-load cargo test --release --test snapshot_load -- --ignored --nocapture

Do not run both legs concurrently or infer an owner warm-return result from this fixture.

## Diagnostics retained, not hidden

- Initial focused compile exits 101: an accidentally removed `info` import, then a test-only MutexGuard borrowing mismatch. Both were corrected before the gated head.
- Initial publication test exit 101 exposed repeated recovery snapshots from the old unread receiver; fresh subscription before recovery capture fixed it.
- Initial HTTP suite exit 101 exposed an existing background debounce not shortening for a newly connected subscriber; the subscribe signal/absolute deadline fixed it. The unchanged existing SSE assertions then passed.
- Initial smoke exit 1 was an evidence-parser issue: routes already returned 200, but ANSI formatting separated field names from `=`. The evidence script now strips ANSI only for assertions, preserving the raw log. Its rerun exited 0.
- The first mutation-driver invocation exited 1 after three successful RED probes: the fourth probe's preflight refused a sibling Xcode run after 180s (admission exit 75), then the driver tried reading its nonexistent test log. No fourth test executed in that attempt; source restoration still succeeded. The driver now checks admission/command status before reading a log and supports an explicit selection. `python3 docs/evidence/issue-555/daemon-mutations.py mutation-no-publication` resumed only the unexecuted probe plus restored controls under the runner/lock, and the wrapper exited 0. No successful RED probe was silently rerun or counted twice.
- Other preflight waits/refusals preceded actual gates while sibling cargo/Xcode work was active. Bounded observations waited for completion/quiet before proceeding; no sibling process was killed or its lock bypassed. These admission exits are not cargo-test failures.

## Delta-first decision and remaining owner work

DELTA-FIRST: skipped the optional new delta-then-snapshot sequence. That would require an additional replay-order contract rather than a trivial reuse. Existing same-epoch delta-only resume is preserved and now reads pre-encoded history; stale/unknown epochs still force an authoritative snapshot. No schema/version change, no changed `Corral-Epoch` request-header meaning, and no changed SSE data framing or keepalive bytes.

NOT EXERCISED: orchestrator's iOS recorded-fixture confirmation; owner Mac warm returns under real fleet load/Tailscale; real git/GitHub/herdr latency distributions; hosted/Linux CI; installed/deployed daemon behavior. The scratch release-binary smoke is not an installed-daemon or owner-gate claim.

NON-CLAIMS: no iOS/Xcode/simulator run by this lane; no deploy, launchctl, live daemon/config change, CI verification, PR confirmation, issue mutation, merge, main-branch change, release or TestFlight.

Cleanup: only the marker-owned `/tmp/g555-daemon-target` was removed after all tests; absence verified, cleanup raw exit 0. `daemon-cleanup.json` and the raw cleanup log record the owner path and disk observation. Hogs and scratch smoke daemons were reaped by their drivers; no sibling build tree was removed. All deliverable source and raw evidence remain in this worktree.

# Issue #555 — round 2 implementation and verification

Source/gate head: `59e0e613bc49784c3608a7b1f9dec5ff9b62d1d5`.
Reviewed head: `60851ed946ae479ce08a114a058ec70b9ee7e61d`.
Pre-publication baseline: `11cc1d26f3b1c22716d4527fb9e7679eaa861aba`.
Worktree: `/Users/jirathip/.herdr/worktrees/corral/impl555-daemon`.
Branch: `g555-daemon-snapshot`.

## Disposition — not an unconditional performance PASS

The single-thread HTTP executor is removed; bounded logging and explicit
subscription-side-effect documentation are implemented. All required cargo
gates, the byte contract, the CPU-hog router budget, and the real-daemon smoke
passed at the source head above.

**The new relative concurrent-read guard FLAGS the fixed head's p99.** Its
measured 5.744708 ms exceeds the predeclared 5.712250 ms bound by 0.032458 ms.
The same guard rejects the reviewed head much more substantially. This flag is
retained, not rounded into a PASS, and the bound was not loosened. A proposed
same-window base/fixed/executor-only-mutation/control experiment did not start:
shared-host admission refused it. There is no new executor-only mutation proof
or clean relative-performance PASS claimed in this round.

**Freshness remains a tradeoff: fast reads can serve data about two seconds
stale.** The new changing-state/CPU-hog measurement observed buffer age p50
856 ms, p99 1984 ms, maximum 2014 ms, versus baseline 1/1/2 ms. An unchanged
buffer can age further without a new mutation being pending. Neither the
2-second debounce nor these observations are a hard real-time OS guarantee.

## F1 — executor removal and corrected disclosure

`src/main.rs:411` now uses the original `axum::serve(listener, app)` on the
normal multi-worker runtime. The entire file is byte-identical to the baseline
at `11cc1d2`; the diff command exits 0. No HTTP runtime split is retained.
Publication's existing `spawn_blocking` implementation is unchanged.

RETRACTED AS AN ACCEPTANCE JUSTIFICATION: the prior report at line 913 said
"Background planes and the publisher cannot occupy its executor queue."
It omitted the consequential fact that ALL HTTP routes shared a single thread.
The old smoke driver did not prove cross-route concurrency. The report's prior
"Status: daemon acceptance criteria verified." conclusion does not establish that
missing property. Its wire and in-process load results remain recorded, but
must not be substituted for actual-main runtime evidence.

On the repaired runtime, background tasks again share runtime workers. The
publisher's encoding remains off that runtime, and cached reads remain
independent of its store/publication locks. This is not a promise that arbitrary
blocking work cannot consume all workers. The smoke driver's misleading
"dedicated HTTP executor" description was corrected to read-readiness only.

The new `round2-http-load.py` executes real release binaries over TCP, not an
in-process router. It records all samples and fails on an exceeded relative
bound. The observed reviewed-head rejection is real; the fixed-head flag and
unexecuted additional attribution proof are explicitly disclosed below.

## F2 — bounded operator diagnostics

- `src/api/read_timing.rs:9-65`: process-wide, separate snapshot/SSE-first-frame
  budgets. Atomic reservation permits at most one aggregate per endpoint per
  five seconds, regardless of clients or concurrent callers.
- `src/api/mod.rs:185` and `src/api/events.rs:19`: replace unconditional info
  logging with those budgets. The records retain `requests`, `max_serve_ms`,
  and `max_buffer_age_ms`; revision/frame metadata is explicitly `latest_*`,
  not falsely associated with the peak request.
- Every observation contributes maxima, including unsampled slow requests.
  The next eligible request emits the aggregate. There is no new idle timer
  or task; a trailing partial window waits for another request. Concurrent
  updates may straddle aggregate windows, as documented in the module.
- The real-handler burst test observed one snapshot info record for 256
  snapshot requests and one SSE-first-frame record for 256 SSE requests.
  The release-binary matrix observed one snapshot aggregate per run, versus
  384 per run at the reviewed head, with the same 384 requests per run.

The two new limiter tests exercise an eight-thread/8000-observation burst and
clock-window/unsampled-peak behavior. They and the public-handler test pass.
The optional old-source replay of the public-handler test was behind the
blocked mutation build and was NOT executed; no assertion-level RED is claimed
for that replay.

## F3 and F4 — explicit semantics, unchanged behavior

`src/core/store.rs:516-520` now begins "Subscribe to live deltas AND wake the
publication coalescer" and explains registration, notification, shortening a
pending background debounce, and the absence of synchronous flushing. This
uses the contract's documentation option; the public name and behavior remain
unchanged.

`src/core/store.rs:40-45` prominently distinguishes fast response time from
roughly two-second stale data and potentially older unchanged buffers. Existing
250 ms foreground / 2 s background debounce and the HTTP test's 3 s publication
deadline remain unchanged.

## Byte equality and changing-state router load

Actual test output:

    G555_BYTE_EQUALITY states=64 snapshots=64 sse_frames=64 empty=1 legacy_header_cases=16 epochs=64 revisions=64

The unchanged test exercises Store publication and the real router. It compares
complete cached JSON bodies and initial SSE frames with independent prior
axum `Json` / `Event::json_data` encoders for the same agent state and capture
time. It includes empty state, randomized revisions/epochs/content, escaping,
and legacy headers; the cache is not its own decoded-state oracle.

The changing-state test is distinct from TCP timing: its clock measures router
service time. Its source hash remains exactly the pre-change baseline's
`08774414aa7681db131a8c4a18f86131832785adab4ad9a2f77384a8e3ebe27b`.

## Actual-main TCP comparison and the retained FLAG

One identical final harness measured all three binaries. The baseline and
reviewed binaries were built from pinned `git archive` sources without
switching this worktree's branch. Every matrix has three fixture runs, each
with 128 `/snapshot` samples in each of three phases:

- idle: no competing history reader;
- single: one continuous `/history?limit=5000` reader;
- concurrent: eight such readers.

Every fixture has 1024 retained history events, no live inputs, a new loopback
connection per request with `Connection: close`, 2-second request deadlines,
and a one-second loaded warmup. Each background reader must progress during
the measured samples. TCP setup/body receipt are timed; JSON inspection is
after that timing. Nearest-rank percentiles are calculated per run, then the
median of the three run percentiles is reported. All samples are retained.

| Binary | Idle p50/p99 ms | Single p50/p99 ms | Concurrent p50/p99 ms |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Baseline `11cc1d2` | 0.193417 / 0.259375 | 0.128500 / 1.016458 | 0.497417 / 1.856125 |
| Reviewed `60851ed` | 0.197334 / 0.327708 | 1.089125 / 1.356375 | 7.844167 / 9.876958 |
| Fixed `59e0e61` | 0.196833 / 0.304292 | 0.190584 / 1.825042 | 1.441292 / 5.744708 |

These are measurements, not an assertion that the fixed numbers meet every
relative criterion. Unchanged-buffer age in these idle-data fixtures reached
19.861 ms at baseline, 3652.472 ms reviewed, and 2762.332 ms fixed. With no new
state mutation, age need not stay below the mutation-to-publication debounce.
The changing-state age table above is the relevant freshness tradeoff.

The relative guard was fixed before measuring the repair:

    candidate concurrent p50 <= 2 * baseline p50 + 1 ms
    candidate concurrent p99 <= 2 * baseline p99 + 2 ms

For the recorded baseline, those bounds are 1.994834117591381 ms and
5.712249919772148 ms. The checker requires matching harness hashes and complete
3-by-128 phase populations before comparing them.

Actual check commands (from the worktree):

    python3 docs/evidence/issue-555/round2-http-load.py check /tmp/g555-r2-http-base-close.json /tmp/g555-r2-http-reviewed.json
    raw exit 1: concurrent /snapshot regression exceeds 2x baseline + 1/2 ms p50/p99

    python3 docs/evidence/issue-555/round2-http-load.py check /tmp/g555-r2-http-base-close.json /tmp/g555-r2-http-fixed.json
    raw exit 1: concurrent /snapshot regression exceeds 2x baseline + 1/2 ms p50/p99

The fixed p50 passes; p99 exceeds its bound by 0.03245808370411396 ms. Neither
result is a green relative gate. The literal issue p99 ceiling of 50 ms is a
separate, looser condition. No tolerance was changed to hide this flag.

would test a time-window variability hypothesis, not assume it true.

measurements or post-mutation controls occurred. The scripts are included for

## #492 Amendment 1 implementation and verification

The preceding STOPPED report is historical and retained byte-for-byte, as
Amendment 1 requires. Implementation is ON TOP OF the accepted report-only
commit `5cb23da21e690bfcb21b346b3231373776012a97`, not a rewrite of it.
Source/test commit: `689139cae7fe4c20d0ddf8c9928ec9d5ae8049eb` (`Refs #492`).
Evidence/documentation are delivered in the subsequent commit. The final
response supplies the verified local/remote delivery SHA; this section does
not try to embed its own future commit hash.

### Changed / scope

- `src/adapters/git_plane.rs:712`: all cache-lock sites recover poison; WARN
  formatting/writing moved outside the lock. `:775`: owned task generations,
  panic/exit and stalled-progress supervision, bounded restart backoff and
  old-generation drain. `:865`: initial/recovered registry hydration.
  `:1371`: separately paced status loop; topology/discovery remain independent.
  `:1444`: per-worktree WARN interval, skipped counter and successful fact ages.
  `:2226`: 200ms probe clock starts AFTER acquiring one of four permits.
- `src/adapters/git_plane_issue492_tests.rs:1`: seven behavioral regressions:
  poison recovery; logged panic/respawn plus real TCP alive/stopped snapshots;
  silent-stall versus healthy-idle control; 100-worktree admission/pacing;
  WARN-subscriber panic outside the cache lock; WARN/counter behavior; and
  stale/absent/stopped facts on published snapshots.
- `src/core/store.rs:66`: shared monotonic health/fact cache. `:358`: publish
  health-only refreshes through the existing coalescer. `:568`: additive
  cached snapshot fields and deterministic stale policy, including unknown
  agent worktree paths. No HTTP-handler reconstruction or filesystem probing.
- `src/core/model.rs:205` / `:233`: additive schema-5 Snapshot fields / GitFactAge.
- `src/main.rs:53`: narrow writer initialization/module registration; `:427`:
  connect the supervised plane to the Store's shared health cache.
- `src/bounded_log.rs:1`: ONE new narrowly named logging module, 194 lines
  including tests. Storage uses stdlib, integrates with the already-present
  tracing writer trait; NO new dependency. Two files, each capped at 1 MiB.
- `tests/model.rs:96`: only additive initialization and assertions (exact diff
  below). No existing assertion was weakened, removed or skipped.
- `.report.md` (this appended section) and `docs/evidence/issue-492/**`:
  commands, receipts, proof drivers, public aggregate measurements and policies.

Protected read-timing/publication/HTTP tests, gh plane, event semantics,
crates, iOS, scripts/installers, workflows and dependency manifests are unchanged.
The original `scope-blocker.md` is unchanged. No worktree pruning occurred.

### Root cause and supervision

The original first panicker cannot be pinned from the retained log: Mac
`corrald-launchd.log:50334` preserves an ENOSPC subscriber-write failure, and
`:50339` is already the SECOND panic (`PoisonError` from the old
`map_event_path` unwrap). This is evidence of the failure boundary, not proof
of the original panicker's exact payload. The regression injects a panicking
WARN subscriber: it now cannot poison the state cache because the lock is
released before logging. Existing poison is recovered on EVERY cache-lock
path, not just the first reported line. Cache observations/pending state are
invalidated and re-probed when a generation is replaced.

A JoinSet owns watcher, topology/discovery, paced status and debounce/retry
children. Panic/unexpected critical exit and 120-second missing loop progress
are observed. The supervisor ERROR-logs the cause, aborts and drains the old
set, waits one second and reinitializes it; a closed sink stops instead of
restarting. One-second idle heartbeats distinguish quiet from stalled.
This does not claim recovery from a non-cooperative OS/runtime deadlock.

Actual captured ERROR (not a synthesized example), from
`logs/g492-mutation-poison-green.log`:

```text
2026-09-16T14:17:13.729407Z ERROR corrald::adapters::git_plane: git plane child failed; restarting generation error=task 27 panicked with message "G492_SUPERVISED_POISON_PAYLOAD"
```

### Wire, stale policy and logging

Fields: `git_plane_alive`; optional `git_plane_last_event_age_ms` (last
responsive loop iteration or successful probe, not the newest content age);
`git_plane_skipped` (all over-budget probes, including suppressed WARNs);
`git_worktree_facts[canonical_path].{fact_age_ms,stale}`. Missing observation
has null age. KEEP historical values, but mark stale when stopped, absent, or
at least 120,000ms old. Metadata is as of `generated_at`, with the existing
up-to-two-second publication cadence; clients retaining snapshots must age
it, not freeze the numeric age. Ordinary deltas do not carry the new top-level
map. No event framing, Corral-Epoch, schema version or client code changed.
README.md in the evidence directory documents the complete consumer contract.

The supervised-poison test serves the real API router over loopback TCP,
fetches an alive snapshot, drops the plane's receiver while keeping the server
running, and fetches a stopped snapshot with increasing age and stale facts.
Its executed marker is `G492_REAL_HTTP_LIVENESS alive=true stopped=false`
(the second boolean is the stopped snapshot's `git_plane_alive` value).
This is distinct from the foreground production-binary cost probes, which
exercise live Herdr/Git inputs without a production fault-injection switch.

WARNs are bounded to one per worktree per five minutes; the exported count
still records every skipped probe. The synchronous writer rotates instead of
refusing at its cap; the panic hook uses that writer. On I/O error it retries
through stderr with a notice and the original bytes; if stderr also fails,
it fail-stops rather than silently claim delivery. This bounds retained file
history, not unlimited retention; emergency stderr is deliberately uncapped.
The two logger tests include an actual subprocess ERROR then panic at the cap
and verify both markers survive, plus oversized writes and poisoned mutex
recovery. No host filesystem was deliberately filled and no real service was
restarted. Existing service log files are not truncated by this lane.

### Biting regression probes and restores

`mutations.py` applies one anchored production mutation at a time, runs the
focused test(s), saves the failure, restores saved bytes with a fresh mtime,
compares SHA-256 and runs `git diff --exit-code HEAD -- <file>` before GREEN.
No compiler error is counted as a mutation RED.

| Production mutation | RED raw exit / observed assertion | Restored GREEN |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Cache helper back to `lock().unwrap()` | 101; 2 tests fail with PoisonError, including supervised path | 0; 7 pass |
| 200ms deadline moved outside permit acquisition | 101; 88 spurious over-budget failures vs expected 0 | 0; 1 pass |
| Remove age/absence stale predicates (keep only stopped) | 101; old fact reported Bool(false) instead of true | 0; 1 pass |

Restores: byte-identical YES for every mutation; each post-restore git diff
exit is 0. `g492-mutations.json` contains each before/after hash and raw exit.
The fake fleet is 100 worktrees, 50ms execution each, max four running,
zero spurious skips at GREEN; the paced run reported 5,995ms within its
6-second scaled interval. No global budget increase or lock suppression.

## Changes and acceptance mapping

- `src/adapters/gh_plane.rs:33,200,675,798`: remove runtime CLI fallback; resolve a nonblank `GITHUB_TOKEN` before an owner-only `github-token` file under `$CORRAL_CONFIG_DIR` or `~/.config/corral`. Missing configuration returns before transport activity, with one `github: disabled (no token)` startup message. File access uses std permissions; group/world permission bits are refused. Keep the SSE subscriber gate, request batching, foreground/background intervals, backoff, and existing plane/data surfaces.
- `src/core/redact.rs:22`: extend GitHub token-family handling. Provider text is sanitized before publication; the active credential is replaced before generic redaction. Branch/head identities are not subjected to the generic high-entropy display-text heuristic, but exact occurrences of the active credential there are still replaced. Synthetic pipeline tests and a response-call mutation prove the sanitizer is load-bearing.
- `src/core/store.rs:61,398,597`: daemon-side binding projection, using #492's existing `GitFactAge.stale` rather than a second clock. With daemon health enabled, missing/stopped/aged facts, or missing both head and branch identity, clear the published `pr_number`, `ci_status`, match source, and associated `issues`. Cached provider facts remain internal for recovery without another GitHub poll. Changes to the projected binding enter the normal revision/delta/resume path. HTTP and initial SSE still share the pre-serialized snapshot. No request-path serialization or filesystem work was added.
- `ios/FleetNotifierTests/FleetNotifierTests.swift:5`: production `WorkspaceLine` raster/OCR regression for unbound, orphan CI, bound PR, and cleared/stale states. `ios/check-release-demo.py:809` changes only the test digest pin and its comment. No production iOS source, badge, settings surface, wire model, project, or release-source pin changed.
- `README.md:19`, `docs/QUICKSTART.md:17`, `docs/LINUX.md:41`, `docs/OPERATIONS.md:48`: install guidance without a GitHub CLI/login prerequisite; optional host-side credentials, precedence, private file permissions and activation documented. No phone token-entry feature.
- `docs/evidence/issue-556/`: real-binary observations, actual GitHub response-derived snapshots, explicitly fictional lane controls, mutation proof, native attachments, raw compressed gate logs, and reproduction instructions.

Protected source/tests, `src/core/model.rs`, `src/main.rs`, HTTP handlers, git-plane files, Cargo manifests/lockfile, Xcode project, and all production iOS sources were byte-unchanged against the base. The protected-path diff command exited 0; the changed-path allowlist audit passed. Existing assertions were not weakened.

### Staleness and publication consequences

Staleness is sampled by the existing publication coalescer, so clearing/recovery inherits its approximately two-second publication delay; it is not an instantaneous UI timer. The current iOS reducer accepts a newer delta revision and replaces each updated agent (`FleetStore.swift:477-485,561-574`), without rejecting the update for an unchanged agent sequence. No iOS production guard was needed.

The new projection and changed-binding tracking add per-publication work over the agent map. #560's existing no-op reserialization problem is NOT fixed, and this additional work can increase coalescer CPU until that follow-up is addressed. No CPU/RSS improvement is claimed. #561's restart rehydration is unchanged.

### Regression discrimination

The mutation driver uses anchored production edits, verifies byte-exact restoration, and records hashes:

1. Permission-mask bypass: `cargo test --lib g556_token_file_permissions_and_rotation -- --nocapture` → 101.
2. Disable stale publication projection: `cargo test --lib g556_binding_only_publishes_with_fresh_git_facts -- --nocapture` → 101.
3. Remove the production response sanitizer call: `cargo test --lib g556_source_processes -- --nocapture` → 101.
4. Restore originals and run `cargo test --lib g556_ -- --nocapture` → 0.

These are assertion failures, not compile failures; they are mutation REDs, not an old-base execution. An earlier development focused run failed due to synthetic fixture literals; that raw log is retained as `rust/focused.log.gz` and is superseded by the corrected focused/restored and full-suite results. No failure was relabelled green.

## No-token run and permissions refusal

`daemon-runs.json` records the exact driver argv and raw exits for all four successful cases. They used the same release binary, SHA-256 `1ce76de61416bc80bd5bcd08986332378f493feb51373ac7f61be97b35e16d32`.

- Mode `none --seconds 300`: actual 300.917 seconds, exit 0, `/usr/bin:/bin` with `gh_on_PATH=false`, two fictional git-backed Herdr agents, and an open SSE subscriber. `none/connections.log` contains 264 `lsof -nP -a -p PID -i` samples, with no non-loopback connection observed. `none/daemon.log` contains exactly one `github: disabled (no token)` and zero GitHub round-trip lines. Agent data and revision were unchanged. Transition-journal size remained 272 bytes, delta 0. Raw snapshots and receipts are in `none/`.
- Sampling does not prove the absence of an arbitrarily short connection between observations. The separate-process transport spy supplies the zero-attempt proof: no transport calls with no token, both before and after an SSE subscriber connects. `generated_at` and fact ages are intentionally excluded from the snapshot-equality assertion.
- Mode `refused --seconds 5`: actual 5.659 seconds, exit 0. An obviously fake credential in a 0644 file produced `github: token file refused (requires owner-only permissions, e.g. 0600)` and then the one disabled line. No GitHub rounds or journal growth. Raw evidence: `refused/`.
- These are driver assertion exits. The disposable daemon children were deliberately terminated afterward (`daemon_termination_exit=-15`), not claimed to have exited naturally with 0.

## Both credential paths and live binding

This is LIVE GitHub data joined to FICTIONAL Herdr lanes and temporary worktrees, not a capture of an existing fleet lane. Public PR: `https://github.com/jirathip-dev/corral/pull/525`, observed OPEN on branch `dependabot/cargo/rust-dependencies-f82fc923d5`; metadata is in `live-pr.json`. The temporary commit is fictional; binding is by the exact branch.

- Env mode: exit 0, 90.987-second observation. A deliberately invalid private-file loser tests environment precedence. Two real successful rounds: `latency_ms=2297 repos=1 changed=1`, then `latency_ms=1780 repos=1 changed=0`; completion interval 61.785596 seconds, including request latency. `env/after.json` has PR 525 and `ci_status: "success"` for p556; p557's unrelated `unbound-556-peer` branch remains null/null.
- File-only mode: exit 0, 91.05 seconds, no `GITHUB_TOKEN` in the daemon environment. Two rounds: `latency_ms=2339 repos=1 changed=1`, then `latency_ms=1842 repos=1 changed=0`; interval 61.84898 seconds. The same bound/null peer assertions pass in `file/after.json`.
- Daemon PATH contains no `gh` in every case. Operator-side credential provisioning was a one-off outside daemon code; its output stayed in memory and was passed only through the driver environment. No real credential is committed, printed, or passed on argv.
- Synthetic fresh/aged/absent/stopped provider facts are explicitly labelled `rust/fixture-*.json`. Tests exercise integration, publication, delta and resume handling, plus recovery without a new provider poll. They also cover fresh health with no Git head/branch identity.

### Disclosed alias limitation and failed first observation

The first env observation exited 1: a matching real PR was polled, but the synthetic Herdr cwd used `/tmp/...` while fresh git facts were keyed by `/private/tmp/...`. The snapshot contained both canonical fresh entries and raw-spelling absent/stale entries. The new guard obeyed that stale metadata and hid the binding. Evidence is retained unchanged under `env-first-alias/`; `first-daemon-runs.json` records the failure.

The fixture was corrected to advertise physical paths; only the env/file observations were then run again. The five-minute observation was not repeated. Alias normalization is NOT fixed: a caller supplying a noncanonical spelling can still lose a fresh binding. This existing #492 metadata-key discrepancy becomes visible as a withheld PR badge under this guard. A follow-up should reconcile identities at ingestion, not add filesystem work to snapshot requests. No git-plane file was edited to hide this result.

## Native rendering and preserved surfaces

`ios/g556-unbound.png`, `g556-unbound-ci.png`, and `g556-stale.png` are byte-identical. `g556-bound.png` visibly contains the #556 PR badge, and the other images do not. The bound and unbound images were visually inspected. They are 1170×144 pixel production-component renders (390×48 points at 3×), not full-screen/device captures. `ios/images.json` records PNG hashes, dimensions and XCTest attachment provenance.

The native badge fixture uses #556; the separate live provider observation uses real PR #525. No claim conflates those sources. All production iOS source remains byte-identical to base.

## Structural and literal verification

- `ast-grep run --lang rust --pattern 'redact_response(&mut $R, $T)' src/adapters/gh_plane.rs --json=compact` recorded three call matches, including the production transport-response boundary; `ast-response.json` preserves the search result.
- `ast-grep run --lang swift --pattern '$A.seq' ios/FleetNotifier/App/FleetStore.swift --json=compact` returned `[]`; focused reading of `accepts` and the delta assignment confirmed revision-based acceptance and unconditional per-ID replacement.
- `grep -rn "gh auth token" src/` → raw exit 1, zero lines.
- `grep -rnF 'Command::new("gh")' src/` → raw exit 1, zero lines.
- The two literal greps are the issue-mandated compatibility fallback; normal exploration used structural search and focused file reads. An initial over-escaped grep spelling was corrected, not used as evidence. `greps.json` contains the actual corrected argv and empty outputs.

## Resource discipline and non-claims

Heavy builds/tests, mutation work, and native boot/test/shutdown were serialized with the shared lock and bounded admission/run deadlines. Two overly conservative lightweight-evidence waiters were cancelled while waiting, before any daemon case executed; the actual observations then reused the already-gated release binary under the lock, with no compilation inside them. Other lanes/processes were not killed. Only this lane's private simulator was erased/deleted; source and evidence were retained.

NOT EXERCISED: owner post-deploy Mac/Bazzite RSS/CPU comparison; Bazzite or Linux execution; installation on a real host physically lacking `gh`; live credential expiry/rotation; a new measurement of the 300-second background interval; physical-device interactions; App Store signing/distribution; hosted CI on the pushed branch. Alias-normalization repair was not attempted. Existing ignored Rust tests and the native opt-in #551 measurement skip remain disclosed.

NON-CLAIMS: no deployment; no restart of any installed daemon; no Bazzite run; no promotion; no PR, merge, issue close, main/release change or TestFlight action; no physical-device claim. Only isolated disposable evidence daemon instances were started and stopped. The owner performance gate remains outstanding.

## #557 Herd git caption — BLOCKED by freshness contract and file fence

Starting head and append base: `d5c2b300d5f174d67d8e72f42970403b0f45fcf2`.
Branch/worktree: `g557-herd-git-signal` at
`/Users/jirathip/.herdr/worktrees/corral/impl557-herd-signal`.
`git fetch origin integration` returned 0; `origin/integration` matched the
starting head. No rebase was needed. Read `.hermes-context.md`, the complete
`.brief.md` including its base-moved amendment, and issue #557 plus comments.

BLOCKER: ios/FleetNotifier/Models/Models.swift:192 — Snapshot discards
`git_worktree_facts`, including each worktree's `stale` flag; the permitted
Herd caption cannot distinguish fresh facts from stale/absent facts — minimal
fix requires an explicitly authorized iOS model/store freshness propagation
change before this caption lane can implement the amended contract.

Evidence and why no caption-only workaround is correct:

- `Models.swift:41-81`: Workspace stores dirty/ahead/behind but no git fact
  freshness or presence. Missing values decode as false/zero; a positive
  dirty/behind value alone is not proof of freshness.
- `Models.swift:181-220`: Snapshot's CodingKeys and decoder retain only
  schema_version, epoch, rev, generated_at, and agents. The additive #492
  git-plane health/fact-age fields are not retained. `Delta:224-238` also
  carries no freshness metadata into the iOS store.
- `HerdModel.swift:47-67,143-151`: HerdHorse contains an Agent plus host
  identity/disconnection. Disconnection describes host transport, not the
  per-worktree git-fact stale predicate. A healthy SSE connection does not
  prove the git plane is alive or that this worktree's fact is fresh.
- `HerdView.swift:3-26,489-517`: the caption receives HerdHorse data, not raw
  snapshots. Adding a helper here cannot recover fields already discarded
  by decoding. Assuming fresh on a connected horse would violate the
  amendment; hiding every signal would violate the positive-fact criterion.
- `src/core/store.rs:597-645` (read only): the snapshot computes the fact-age
  map and publishes it separately. Its stale-binding filter at 628-632
  clears PR/CI/issues, NOT workspace.dirty or workspace.behind. Therefore
  the #556 stale CI rule cannot be treated as a dirty/behind sanitizer.
  `GitFactAge::at` at 109-115 marks absent/stopped/age >= 120000 ms stale;
  the roughly two-second publication cadence is not this predicate.

Required scope decision: permit existing iOS model/store/projection sources
outside this fence to decode and preserve authoritative per-worktree
freshness through snapshot/delta and per-host retained-row handling, then
resume this lane; alternatively land that prerequisite separately. No daemon
or wire-schema change is proposed here. Do not reinterpret transport liveness
or invent an age threshold in HerdView. No forbidden file was changed.

Changes: this append-only report section only. No implementation, test,
release-digest, Board-card, schema, project or evidence-image change. Prior
report bytes are preserved; the original file SHA-256 was
`a8fb99c995d46aac598db7f6e5e653491730a13a9b4b6172a786625491192ec4`.

## #558 Recent-output sheet worktree block — scope blocker receipt

Starting HEAD and fetched `origin/integration`:
`74c96a1d57528083e67df7fd24de9764e21373f9`.
Worktree: `/Users/jirathip/.herdr/worktrees/corral/impl558-sheet-block`.
Branch: `g558-sheet-worktree-block`. `git rebase origin/integration` returned
0 without moving HEAD (already current). No implementation changes were made.

BLOCKER: `ios/FleetNotifier/Models/Models.swift:41-82` — the iOS Workspace
also drops `head_sha` and `head_subject`, not just `issues`. The Line-2
requirement cannot be implemented within the current fence. Minimal fix:
authorize two additional optional String fields, `headSha` and `headSubject`,
with nil initializer defaults, explicit `head_sha` / `head_subject` CodingKeys,
and `decodeIfPresent`, alongside the already-authorized optional issues field.
This would be client-only, additive, and require no daemon/wire/schema change.
Older-payload tests must cover all three omitted fields when work resumes.

### Evidence and scope decision

- The daemon already declares both values at `src/core/model.rs:132,138`.
  The client Workspace declares neither property nor either CodingKey and
  decodes only repo, branch, worktree_path, pr_number, ci_status, dirty, ahead,
  behind (`Models.swift:42-49,51-56,72-82`).
- The actual sheet obtains a typed Agent through `model.fleetAgent(...)`
  (`FleetViews.swift:5787`; `AppModel.swift:3927-3931`). Snapshot decoding
  uses `JSONDecoder().decode(Snapshot.self, from: data)`
  (`CorraldClient.swift:114`); `Agent` decodes the typed Workspace at
  `Models.swift:166`. No raw workspace payload reaches the sheet.
- Structural searches `ast-grep run --lang swift --pattern '$OBJECT.headSha'
  ios/FleetNotifier` and the corresponding `$OBJECT.headSubject` search
  each returned raw exit 1 with no matches. A subsequent text search across
  `ios/**/*.swift` for `head_sha|head_subject|headSha|headSubject|git_worktree_facts`
  found only a forbidden-token assertion in `FleetNotifierTests/HerdTests.swift:21`,
  not another decoder or retained field. `ast-grep outline src/core/model.rs
  --match 'Workspace|GhIssueRef' --view expanded` showed both daemon fields
  at the lines cited above. These are source inspection, not runtime tests.
- The latest brief explicitly authorizes Models.swift ONLY for the optional
  issues field (`.brief.md:85-90,144-147`) and requires STOP when acceptance
  needs an edit outside that fence. No attempt was made to hide new decoding
  in UI helpers, infer a commit from tail text, fetch independently, or
  substitute fixture-only data for unavailable production data.
- The daemon-side stale-binding filter IS present at `src/core/store.rs:620-633`:
  with git-plane health enabled, a stale/missing age or missing SHA-and-branch
  clears PR, CI, match source and issues. This corroborates the brief's new
  routing; it is not the blocker. No client fixture or stale-binding runtime
  test was executed, and no claim of full stale-case acceptance is made.

## #539 — curl-only public installer (bounded implementer delivery)

The delivery commit adds this archive entry and evidence only; its HEAD SHA is
reported in the lane's final message. Nothing is pushed or merged.

Reproduce from the worktree with `python3 docs/evidence/issue-539/run-proof.py`.
The completed invocation exited 0. Commands have deadlines (240 seconds per
local operation; 300 seconds for the remote proof). No test command was piped
through a filter to infer PASS. `commands.json` records driver argv, environment,
raw exits and working directories. `scenario-results.json` records EVERY installer
scenario's expanded command, raw exit and actual diagnostics; `macos.log.gz` and
`bazzite.log.gz` are the unabridged transcripts, including HTTP curl argv and PATH
lookups. Read gzip text with Python's `gzip.open(path, "rt")`.

## Results

- macOS host: `Darwin arm64`; `/bin/bash scripts/test-install-corral-linux.sh`
  exit 0, 1275 executed assertions,
  113 installer invocations (100 resolution cases,
  13 preserved-behavior cases). Both Linux-dispatch and native Darwin-dispatch
  installer paths ran; macOS `plutil` checked generated daemon plists.
- Bazzite host: `Linux x86_64`; same command, exit 0,
  671 executed assertions,
  64 recorded invocations (50 resolution cases,
  13 preserved-behavior cases and `systemd-analyze verify`, itself exit 0).
- Installer fixture binaries are deliberately inert; systemctl, launchctl and
  health checks are stubbed. This proves resolution, checksums, staging and the
  existing service-helper integration, not that a real daemon binary was launched.
- True pre-fix control: exact base installer + new suite in a disposable scripts
  tree exited 1; first installer exited 97 at poison `gh api`, rather than 0.
  `base-forbidden.log` records the real intercepted command. The suite had
  completed 1 assertion before rejecting `test 97 -eq 0`. The lane's sources were
  never swapped; all 7 copied script hashes matched the tested tree afterwards.

## gh absence versus poison shim

An executable poison `gh` and empty `command -v gh` cannot coexist on the SAME
PATH. The suite therefore installs the poison shim first and exercises two
honest environments rather than hiding that contradiction:

1. Poison leg: `<work>/poison-bin:<work>/stub-bin:/usr/bin:/bin`.
   `command -v gh` exits 0 and prints exactly `<work>/poison-bin/gh`.
   The shim logs then exits 97 if invoked. The cumulative forbidden log remains
   empty while latest/tag/URL installs succeed.
2. Absent leg: `<work>/stub-bin:/usr/bin:/bin`.
   Non-login `/bin/bash --noprofile --norc -c 'command -v gh'` exits 1 with
   EMPTY stdout, checked inside every case. Installs still succeed.

`macos-forbidden.log` and `bazzite-forbidden.log` are actual saved 0-byte logs;
no gh/cargo/rustc/git invocation occurred in the successful suites. Credentials
are absent because each installer uses `env -i` and a fake HOME. A fixture brew
path prevents Darwin setup's existing PATH helper from introducing host Homebrew.
The local `gh issue view` used for brief discovery is NOT part of these test
environments or a claim that gh is uninstalled on either host.
