## Dependency and scope contracts

Resolver before:
```
tag="$(gh api "repos/$RELEASE_REPO/$endpoint" --jq '.tag_name')"
asset_list="$(gh api "repos/$RELEASE_REPO/releases/tags/$tag" --jq '.assets[] | [.name, .browser_download_url] | @tsv')"
```
Resolver/download after (one shared HTTP helper):
```
fetch_release "https://github.com/$RELEASE_REPO/releases/latest" /dev/null --head
curl -q -sS --connect-timeout 15 --max-time 300 --max-redirs 10 \
  "$mode" --dump-header "$WORK_DIR/http.headers" --output "$output" \
  --write-out '%{http_code}' "$url"
ASSET_URL="https://github.com/$RELEASE_REPO/releases/download/$tag/$ASSET_NAME"
CHECKSUM_URL="$ASSET_URL.sha256"
fetch_release "$ASSET_URL" "$BUNDLE_FILE" --location
fetch_release "$CHECKSUM_URL" "$CHECKSUM_FILE" --location
```
Latest uses the website's tag redirect; pinned tags form release asset URLs
without discovery; pinned URLs still append `.sha256`. `--release`/`--url`
parsing and mutual exclusion are unchanged. Slash-to-underscore asset naming
matches the existing release workflow. No JSON endpoint remains.

403/429 refuse immediately, preserving Retry-After (seconds or HTTP date) and
X-RateLimit-Reset in the loud diagnostic. There is NO automatic retry or sleep:
the installer does not send a request ahead of the server's retry boundary and
instructs the caller to wait. Headers are case-insensitive and only the final
HTTP response counts. Downloads also use this classifier, so an error after
resolution is not hidden. Curl ignores curlrc (`-q`) and has bounded connect,
request and redirect limits. Offline/DNS/TLS failures report the original curl
exit; missing releases/assets, HTTP errors and SHA mismatch are distinguishable.
No fallback source-build path was added.

Production dependency set does not grow: Bash + curl + existing Unix tools
(awk, basename, dirname, sed, tr, mktemp, tar, grep, head, id, uname, mkdir, rm,
mv, chmod, and existing platform service/helper utilities). SHA-256 still uses
shasum OR sha256sum OR openssl, unchanged. Resolver adds NO jq/Python/Rust/git or
GitHub authentication; removes gh. awk/mktemp/rm were already installer
requirements. Python/SSH/ShellCheck here are proof-driver tools, not installation
requirements.

Production diff: scripts/install-corral.sh +71/-29;
scripts/test-install-corral-linux.sh +276/-41. All other changes are this
issue's evidence and the append-only report entry. `scripts/update-corral.sh`,
setup helpers, runtime GitHub code, iOS, crates, src and workflows are byte
unchanged. No new automatic updater or packaging framework was introduced;
existing release setup behavior was preserved rather than changed out of scope.

Report prefix at base: 188196 bytes,
SHA-256 `4cafd683386e25247759f40d1a6c1d81ffb17b1120afd2203b62023360365537`. The closeout verifies this entire
prefix against the final archive, checks the changed-path fence and runs
`git diff --check` and `git status --porcelain`. File/line inventory is
`files.txt`; final archive append/status receipt is `closeout.json`.

NON-CLAIMS: no device evidence, no hosted-CI claim, no merge, no push, no release,
no runtime-GitHub change, no authenticated runtime/core-board audit, no live
public asset download, and no real daemon launch. These are hermetic installer
proofs on two actual hosts. Stopping at the brief's fence.

## #539 curl-only installer merge entry (2026-09-17)

Branch `g539-curl-install`, reviewed head `79a4bf2dc3b8523dde1886da1023546b78480c86` (implementation
`d588c98`, evidence `79a4bf2`), merged into `integration` `05dafa0801c264de193e3f518914738c5ed1357d`.

The installer no longer needs GitHub CLI. `resolve_release_urls()` used `gh api` twice (lines 341/347);
it now resolves through the public website's `releases/latest` redirect and constructs release asset URLs
deterministically (`https://github.com/<repo>/releases/download/<tag>/<asset>`), so no JSON endpoint, no
anonymous API quota and no GitHub credential are involved. `require_command gh` is removed and the
`require_command` set shrank from three to two with nothing added. One shared `fetch_release()` helper does
every HTTP call with `curl -q` (proven load-bearing: a hostile `~/.curlrc` hijacks the output without it and
does not with it), bounded connect/request/redirect limits, `--dump-header` plus `--write-out '%{http_code}'`,
and an explicit classifier: 403/429 refuse immediately and echo `Retry-After` (seconds and HTTP-date forms) and
`X-RateLimit-Reset` with no automatic retry; 404/410 are distinguished; network/TLS failures report the
original curl exit; other statuses are refused. There is no source-build fallback anywhere on the failure path.

INDEPENDENT REVIEW: PASS with zero blocking findings, at the exact head, worktree pristine.
**DISCLOSURE, and a correction:** the reviewer self-reported `DISCLOSURE: primary (model glm-5.3-flash; no
fallback served this review)`. That attestation is FALSE and I am correcting it rather than repeating it: the
profile log for that exact session (`20260917_073325_f6d4ea`) shows `API call failed ... provider=anthropic
model=claude-opus-5 ... HTTP 429` followed by `Fallback to zai/glm-5.3-flash`, and every subsequent API call in
the session is `model=glm-5.3-flash provider=zai`. The profile still declares `claude-opus-5`/`anthropic`, and the
sibling `fleet-rev` session (`20260917_073538_ce7fa0`) fell back identically. **This gate was served by the
configured fallback model, account-level after an Anthropic rate limit — the merge is therefore
warrant-degraded and is recorded as such.** The implementer lane itself ran on its primary (`gpt-6-astra`).

What the reviewer independently derived rather than accepted, and why it matters here: it red-probed the very
thing that would have made the "gh was never invoked" proof vacuous — the poison `gh` shim genuinely records
(a deliberate `gh api ...` on that PATH returned 97 and grew the forbidden log to 64 bytes), and it re-derived
the base RED itself (base installer + delivered suite → suite exit 1). It then mutated the new classifier three
ways (403/429 branch to `return 0`; `Retry-After` lookup pointed at a nonexistent header; 404/410 branch deleted)
and each mutation produced a specific RED with a byte-identical restore, hash-checked per leg. It extracted
`response_header()` verbatim and ran twelve parsing probes (CRLF/LF, case variants, proxy preamble, absent
header, obs-fold, tab after colon, leading space) and confirmed `Location` acceptance is exact-prefix only, with
evil-host and other-repo redirect targets refused (exit 5). It proved the failure path end-to-end with an
all-refusing curl (exit 6, no install root created, no `~/.local`, `TMPDIR` honoured, zero leaked temp dirs) and
scanned the `+276/-41` test diff line by line confirming **zero assertions deleted or weakened** (the deletions
are a fixture rewrite, a logging wrapper around unchanged invocations, and a strengthened final echo) with
checksum-before-install, platform refusal, managed service, rollback (including a new rollback-of-an-existing-
release scenario) and key preservation all still asserted. It verified surviving evidence arithmetic
(113/64 invocations, 1275/671 assertions reproduce; exits.tsv matches records 1:1) and the argument-parse region
is byte-identical to base.

Bazzite isolation, corroborated against the live host read-only: the shipped before/after manifests are equal
(5767 entries, digest `aae33212…`, service active, `MainPID` and monotonic activation timestamp identical), the
manifest function was proven SENSITIVE on a scratch copy (a one-byte content change, a chmod-only change and a
symlink retarget each move the digest), and a fresh read-only recompute today found the same four roots with
exactly 5767 entries and zero path-level differences, `corrald` still active on the SAME `MainPID`, and all 5053
regular files byte-identical except exactly three daemon-owned runtime logs the live service appended to — the
signature of an untouched installation with a continuously running daemon. Nothing on that host was modified by
the lane or the reviewer.

ORCHESTRATOR BATTERY at the same head (the merge gate, run by me, independently of the lane's drivers): the
hermetic suite on macOS with a poison `gh` shim of my own on PATH, the same suite on Bazzite in an isolated
remote scratch root with `gh` poisoned, `shellcheck` on both scripts, and a structural check that no `gh`

### Test mechanism and native execution commands

`ThemeStoreTests.testForeignSuiteWriteDoesNotRereadOwnDefaults` injects a
real `UserDefaults` subclass on a unique own suite. The spy overrides the
real `string(forKey:)` read, counts it under a lock, and calibrates that
initialization actually reads. A real `set` on a different unique suite
must emit its own notification (positive expectation), while a 1.2-second
inverted expectation and read-count equality require no own-suite re-read.
No notification is manually posted and no source-text assertion supplies
the behavioral proof. The store is kept alive through the observation.
The spy consumes its read expectation once because refresh reads two keys.

The existing `testExternalEnvironmentWriteRefreshesTheResolvedPalette`
remains byte-unchanged and checks the complementary same-suite behavior.
The delivered `ThemeTests.swift` SHA-256 is identical in the exact-base RED
and the GREEN runs:

## #533 theme-store observer scoping merge entry (2026-09-17)

Branch `g533-theme-scope`, reviewed head `b587a72f204fad8a489e7531809777e4a0e829e2` (kept fence receipt `f65edb4`,
implementation `e55974d`, evidence `b587a72`), merged into `integration`.

The theme store's `UserDefaults` observer is scoped to its own suite: `object: nil` becomes `object: defaults` at
`ios/FleetNotifier/UI/AppTheme.swift:380`. Before this change a write to ANY suite woke the observer and re-read the
store's own values; benign in the observed population (values change only on real differences) but latent
shared-state coupling introduced by #526. This lane existed because that finding was authorized earlier and never
implemented - no commit referenced it.

WHY THE FIX NEEDED A PIN AMENDMENT (orchestrator error, corrected in-loop): `AppTheme.swift` is manifest entry
`ios/release_source_manifest.py:56` of the release-source set whose whole-set digest is pinned at
`ios/check-release-demo.py:573`. Any edit to that file therefore re-pins it, and the original fence excluded the pin
file, so the lane correctly stopped report-only before touching production code. Amendment 1 authorized the
release-source pin ONLY (constant + comment, recomputed with the repository's own `release_source_digest`, proven in
both directions). `APPROVED_TEST_SOURCE_DIGEST` did NOT move, because the tests went into the unpinned
`ThemeTests.swift` rather than the pinned `FleetNotifierTests.swift` - the preferred home named in the amendment.

RED/GREEN, re-derived independently by the reviewer at the exact base `05dafa08` in a detached worktree with only
the test file overlaid: `red_exit=65`, 17 tests, 2 failures, both in
`testForeignSuiteWriteDoesNotRereadOwnDefaults` - `XCTWaiterResult(rawValue: 4)` vs `(rawValue: 1)` and read-count

stays pinned by `testExternalEnvironmentWriteRefreshesTheResolvedPalette`, which still BITES (mutating

green).

The reviewer's most important answer (this was the orchestrator's primary adversarial target): the scoping does NOT

default (`FleetNotifierApp.swift:17` → `AppTheme.swift:337`), `@AppStorage("herdEnvironment")`

## #539 Amendment 1 — CI portability fix: gh-free PATH by construction

Branch: `g539-curl-install`.
Round starting HEAD: `79a4bf2dc3b8523dde1886da1023546b78480c86` (the prior delivery, unchanged).
Original issue base: `05dafa0801c264de193e3f518914738c5ed1357d`.

`a42b83ab3e50aa9553076971f39176711f20cb76`.
This round adds ONE new local commit on the existing branch. Its SHA is the
final lane message; no prior commit is amended, rebased or rewritten.

### Correction and bounded change

The earlier macOS/Bazzite runs did not prove the absence premise on Ubuntu.
The amendment's actual hosted failure was:
```
PATH=/tmp/tmp.fWODKeHczb/stub-bin:/usr/bin:/bin; command -v gh output=</usr/bin/gh>; RAW_EXIT=0
FAIL: assertion failed: test 0 -eq 1 (completed assertions: 283)
```
Those earlier local results remain archived, not rewritten or promoted to a
hosted-CI claim. The production resolver was not implicated and is untouched.

Only `scripts/test-install-corral-linux.sh` changes (+39/-0), plus this
APPEND-ONLY report entry. No new evidence files are added to the repository
under the amendment's tighter fence; named raw diagnostic logs are listed below.
The owner-supplied untracked `.amend-1.md` is retained, not committed or removed.

`make_gh_free_bin(destination, source_path)` symlinks only explicit tool names.
The absent leg now uses `$STUB_BIN:$ABSENT_BIN`; neither `/usr/bin` nor `/bin`
is appended. `/bin` can expose the same gh as `/usr/bin` on usrmerged Linux,
so it is NOT treated as a safe fallback. Absolute symlink targets provide tools
without exposing their siblings through PATH.

The tool set was derived with:
```
ast-grep run --lang bash --kind command --json=compact scripts/install-corral.sh scripts/setup-corrald.sh scripts/setup-corrald-linux.sh scripts/lib-corral-update-path.sh
```
Raw exit 0; map saved as `/tmp/corral539-amend1-command-map.json`.
The installer and release helpers use bash/curl/awk/sed/tr/tar/mktemp/basename/
dirname/grep/head/id/uname/mkdir/rm/mv/chmod/cat/cmp/sleep, with the existing
shasum/sha256sum/openssl alternatives and Darwin plutil. The curl fixture adds
cp; gzip is included for GNU tar's compression subprocess. Service commands,
source-build tools and brew continue to resolve to the existing fixture stubs.
No production dependency was added. Optional tools absent on a platform are
not linked; required-tool failures cannot fall through to a host PATH.

The poison leg is BYTE-UNCHANGED, including its
`$POISON_BIN:$STUB_BIN:/usr/bin:/bin` PATH, shim and assertions. Every old
assertion remains byte-identical. A reconstruction check removed ONLY the new
farm block and one new absent-PATH assignment in memory, then compared the
entire suite with the starting commit: exact equality (raw exit 0).
`git diff --numstat` confirmed 39 additions and ZERO deletions.

## #539 CI fix merge entry — runner-parity repair (2026-09-17)

Follow-up commit `714e998ca818845958b8b1311c9af8c5f96a9fa7` on `g539-curl-install`, fixing the hosted-CI failure of
the already-merged `79a4bf2dc3b8523dde1886da1023546b78480c86`. Test-only: `scripts/install-corral.sh` is
byte-identical to the merged head.

WHY: the `Linux installer gate` failed on `ubuntu-latest` at the merged head:

    PATH=/tmp/…/stub-bin:/usr/bin:/bin; command -v gh output=</usr/bin/gh>; RAW_EXIT=0
    FAIL: assertion failed: test 0 -eq 1 (completed assertions: 283)

The suite's gh-absent leg asserted a property of the HOST — that `gh` is absent from `/usr/bin:/bin`. That holds on
macOS (no `gh`) and on a Linux host whose `gh` lives in `~/.local/bin`, which is why the implementer lane, the
independent reviewer AND the orchestrator's own two-host battery all passed locally while the runner (which ships
`gh` at `/usr/bin/gh`) failed. Three independent local runs could not see it because none of them exercised the
premise. The production resolver was never implicated: in the same job `forbidden invocations: 0`, the poison-bin
leg passed, and the checksum-mismatch scenario still produced its class-distinct refusal with exit 1.

THE FIX makes the premise true BY CONSTRUCTION. `make_gh_free_bin` builds a symlink farm over only the tool names
the installer, its helpers and the suite's own stubs need (`bash curl awk sed tr tar gzip mktemp basename dirname
grep head id uname mkdir rm mv chmod cat cmp cp sleep shasum sha256sum openssl plutil`), skipping anything absent
(the hash tools are alternatives; `plutil` is macOS-only), and the absent leg now uses `$STUB_BIN:$ABSENT_BIN`
instead of `/usr/bin:/bin`. Crucially it also SIMULATES THE RUNNER ON EVERY HOST: it builds `RUNNER_BIN` from
`/usr/bin:/bin`, copies a `gh` into it, asserts `gh` resolves there, then builds `ABSENT_BIN` from that contaminated
source and asserts `gh` is gone (`! -e`, `! -L`, `command -v gh` exit 1 with empty stdout). A bypass probe turns
the suite RED, so the farm check demonstrably bites. No assertion was weakened: the added block plus the one-line
path override reconstruct the baseline byte-for-byte (`ALL_PRIOR_SUITE_BYTES_PRESERVED=true`,
`POISON_LEG_AND_ASSERTIONS_BYTE_UNCHANGED=true`).

HOSTED VERDICT (the authority, since local proof is only a proxy): the gate was re-run on the fix branch and
`Secret scan`, `iOS art/icon gates` and **`Linux installer gate` are all SUCCESS at `714e998`**.

ORCHESTRATOR BATTERY at the fix head: macOS full suite exit 0 with **1283 assertions** and
`forbidden invocations: 0`, run with a `gh` planted in a directory the OLD premise trusted and never invoked;
Bazzite isolated suite exit 0 with **679 assertions** and 0 forbidden invocations, live install untouched
(`MainPID 3887997`), remote scratch removed; `shellcheck` 0 on both scripts; `git diff --check` clean; head
unchanged after the battery. Orchestrator also independently cross-checked the farm's coverage against the tools
the installer and its helpers actually invoke: the apparent extras (`install`, `date`, `stat`, `env`, `git`, `find`)
are false positives — label strings or absent entirely — and the one real extra (`launchctl`, macOS service
removal at `install-corral.sh:252-253`) is stubbed by the suite alongside `systemctl` and `curl`.

## #560 — hard-fence stop: metadata-only refresh needs publication-encoder scope

Disposition: BLOCKED BY SCOPE, not an implementation delivery. Stopped before
adding tests or changing production code. The defect is not fixed. The brief
explicitly requires stopping when a requirement appears to need an out-of-fence
change; this entry records that stop, not a request to silently widen the fence.

Worktree: `/Users/jirathip/.herdr/worktrees/corral/impl560-publishskip`.
Branch: `g560-publishskip`.
Audited HEAD before this report-only commit: `a42b83ab3e50aa9553076971f39176711f20cb76`.
Brief base: `a42b83ab3e50aa9553076971f39176711f20cb76` (exact match).
The final response supplies the report-only delivery commit SHA; the audited
Rust HEAD remains the base above, not an unimplemented candidate fix.
Initial `git status --porcelain`: empty. Read the complete brief and issue #560
(body plus all three returned comments). No root/ancestor AGENTS.md or CLAUDE.md,
justfile or Makefile was found; no recipe or host setup was invented.

### Blocking boundary and minimum authorization

BLOCKER: `src/core/store/published.rs:23-36`, specifically `:29`, is the existing
immutable snapshot constructor and is outside the exact allowed production-file
list (`src/core/store.rs`, `src/main.rs`). Every non-skipped `flush()` calls it at
`src/core/store.rs:462`; it always invokes `wire_bytes(epoch, snapshot)`.
`src/core/store/published.rs:72-81` converts the entire Snapshot, including its
agent map, to a serde_json::Value and then encodes the entire object. The
publication fields at `:12-20` contain whole body/event buffers, not a separately
reusable encoded agent map. These are static source findings, NOT measured
serialization counts or CPU evidence.

Required proof item 4 says that, if keeping dead-plane ages fresh requires
publication, publish only what changed and prove the agent map is not
re-serialized. The existing contract at `docs/evidence/issue-492/README.md:37-55`
requires these published ages to refresh within the up-to-two-second cadence.
Changing ages changes the immutable JSON body. Merely deleting `!enabled` from
the skip guard freezes that metadata on a stopped plane. Bucketing full
publications within the existing freshness bound still sends the whole map
through the constructor above at every age refresh; a longer bucket would
relax the documented bound. Neither fulfills proof item 4 as written.

The bounded, byte-preserving repair appears to require a reusable encoded-agent
component in the publication encoder, plus store-side invalidation when agent
content or its stale projection changes. That means authorizing
`src/core/store/published.rs` for this narrow optimization. Do not evade the
boundary by relocating/duplicating the wire encoder or byte-splicing whole
published JSON inside store.rs. A separate health-only SSE event would instead
change the expressly forbidden wire/schema. No such workaround was attempted.
This is not a claim that no conceivable algorithm can be placed in store.rs;
it is a stop at the existing encoder ownership and the explicit wire fence.

Minimum next-brief amendment: allow the byte-preserving cache/composition work
in `src/core/store/published.rs`, keeping schema, field semantics, SSE framing,
HTTP request behavior and #555a's independent byte oracle unchanged; retain
store/publication tests within the already allowed test scope. Alternatively,
explicitly change proof item 4 if periodic full-map encoding is acceptable.
No adapter, client, installer, live service, or dependency change is requested.

### Guard before / after

Unchanged at `src/core/store.rs:389-395` (comments omitted here):

```rust
if !inner.pending_any
    && !self.git_plane_health.enabled.load(Ordering::Acquire)
    && previous.git_plane_backlog == self.git_plane_backlog.load(Ordering::Acquire)
{
    return None;
}
```

No after-fix guard exists. No performance improvement or freshness fix is claimed.

No gate pin for `src/core/store.rs`, `src/main.rs`, or a Rust-source-set digest
was found. The pin search itself is NOT the blocker. Exact `search_files` tool
invocations, patterns, scopes and untruncated returned matches are retained in

## #560 — AMENDMENT 1: skip unchanged publications and reuse encoded agents

### Identity and scope

- Tested source HEAD: `7696af2a83f64df7ae9841284a9a7eae20df1d45`.
- Branch: `g560-publishskip`.
- Base: `a42b83ab3e50aa9553076971f39176711f20cb76`.
- Receipt commit retained as an ancestor: `4e6d185a257ad137c857244d96ce5194cd8c2a34`.
- This entry APPENDS to the accepted original fence-stop receipt. Amendment 1
  authorizes the encoder change below; it does not weaken the acceptance criteria.
  The rejected alternative, periodic full-map encoding for unchanged agents, is
  NOT implemented.
- Production/test diff against base: `src/core/store.rs` +16/-11;
  `src/core/store/published.rs` +101/-12;
  `src/core/store/publication_tests.rs` +335/-0. All other additions are this
  append-only report and `docs/evidence/issue-560/**`; the closing file inventory
  below records the evidence line counts. The delivery commit adds evidence only;
  its final SHA is supplied in the final response rather than self-referenced here.
- `src/main.rs`, model/schema, HTTP/SSE handlers, adapters, manifests/lockfile,
  `ios/**`, `scripts/**`, and `.github/**` are unchanged. No new dependency.

### Guard and encoder

Before:

```rust
!inner.pending_any
    && !self.git_plane_health.enabled.load(Ordering::Acquire)
    && previous.git_plane_backlog == self.git_plane_backlog.load(Ordering::Acquire)
```

After:

```rust
let same_agents = snapshot.agents == previous.agents.state;
if !inner.pending_any && same_agents && previous.same_health(&snapshot) {
    return None;
}
let cached_agents = same_agents.then(|| previous.agents.clone());
```

The comparison uses the actual stale-projected map, not only adapter dirty flags.
A content update, remove/reinsert, expiry, stop or recovery invalidates the encoded
component when its visible agent content changes. A no-op upsert retains revision
semantics but reuses the component. Health comparison includes backlog, alive,
skipped count, last-event age and every path's age/stale status. Known relative
ages also retain their time-origin dependency: equal age numbers from a newer
observation cannot indefinitely retain an old `generated_at`.

`EncodedAgents::new` is the single full-map encoding site. It keeps the immutable
projected map and its encoded JSON value together in an Arc. The encoder takes the
agent map out of the metadata Snapshot, serializes only that small metadata, and
composes a NEW complete JSON object with the reusable encoded agent value. This is
inside the existing publication encoder, not JSON slicing or an encoder copy in
`store.rs`. Delta bytes still use the same encoder; SSE framing is unchanged.

Precisely what is still published on age-only ticks: a complete snapshot body
and initial-SSE buffer, with fresh metadata and the cached agent JSON value. There
is no health-only SSE event, invented delta or invented revision. Ordinary deltas
retain their existing schema. Full-buffer allocation/copy and projected-map
cloning/comparison remain; this is NOT a zero-allocation or bandwidth-reduction
claim. The expensive full-map serialization is eliminated for unchanged agents.

The owned publication guard still spans capture, blocking encoding and atomic
watch-pointer replacement. Replacement still precedes broadcasting the delta.
Readers retain immutable old buffers; HTTP handlers do not call `flush()`.

### Observed RED/GREEN and encoder proof

Both runs invoked the same Cargo command from this worktree:

```text
cargo test -p corrald g560_unchanged_ticks_keep_publication_identity -- --nocapture
```

The test retains the old Arc while comparing `Arc::as_ptr` after each of ten
`flush()` ticks spaced by `FOREGROUND_TICK` (250ms), with 200 seeded agents. These
are direct invocations of the coalescer's publication operation, not ten HTTP
requests or ten emitted SSE events. The unchanged-state fixture has no known
fact/event ages. A separate real-coalescer test covers advancing known ages.

- RED, `runs/red-prefixed.log` / `.json`: raw exit **101**, 0 passed / 1 failed.
  Observed health OFF **0/10**, health ON **10/10** republications.
  Assertion at `publication_tests.rs:394:5`:
  `unchanged health-enabled ticks must not republish`; left `[0, 10]`, right `[0, 0]`.
- RED was captured BEFORE production changes. Git HEAD then was the report-only
  receipt `4e6d185`; its production sources are byte-identical to pre-fix `a42b83ab`.
  This is not a claim that the worktree was detached at `a42b83ab`.
  `git diff --exit-code a42b83ab3e50aa9553076971f39176711f20cb76
  4e6d185a257ad137c857244d96ce5194cd8c2a34 -- src crates Cargo.toml Cargo.lock`
  returned **0** (`runs/red-production-identity.*`).
- GREEN at the tested source HEAD, `runs/green-regression.*`: raw exit **0**,
  1 passed / 0 failed; observed OFF **0/10**, ON **0/10**.
- `cargo test -p corrald g560_ -- --nocapture`: raw exit **0**, **6 passed** /
  0 failed (`runs/green-focused.*`). Filtered binaries' zero-test results are not
  counted as additional executed tests.
- The encoder-thread-local counter is incremented at the actual full-map
  serialization site, not inferred from a publication flag. Each new publication
  records the local before/after difference, so concurrent tests cannot pollute it.
  Dead-plane real coalescer: **3/3** new publication Arcs, **0/3** full-map encodes.
  The immutable encoded-agent Arc remains identical across all three publications.
- `g560_cached_encoder_matches_independent_bytes` additionally exercises **64**
  metadata variants with **0** agent-map encodes while checking **64** snapshot
  bodies and **64** complete SSE frames against the independent old encoder.
- The invalidation test checks real upsert/no-op/update/remove/reinsert and
  expiry/fresh-again/stopped/recovered transitions. Actual changed-map paths record
  **1** encode; the no-op records **0**. Stale projection deltas carry the expected
  PR binding removal/restoration. Equal-age/new-origin behavior has its own test.

### Freshness and real delivery

`g560_dead_plane_ages_reuse_agent_encoding` starts the REAL coalescer with 200
agents, a frozen last observation and `alive=false`; no plane task makes progress.
Across three background ticks, observed fact ages were **12011, 14035, 16061ms**;
last-event ages also strictly increased, alive stayed false and stale stayed true.
The observed waits were **2023, 2021, 2010ms**, with zero agent-map encodes each.
The middle iteration sends a late wake at 1250ms: it cannot restart another whole
background window. The scheduler now keeps an absolute idle-refresh deadline.

`g560_real_change_reaches_http_and_sse_within_bound` uses real store writes and the
real router. With no SSE subscriber, the new publication arrived in **2003ms** and
`GET /snapshot` returned its exact cached bytes. After opening `/events` and
consuming its initial snapshot, another real title change arrived as the expected
live delta in **253ms**, and the current snapshot carried that title.

The policy remains the existing **2s background / 250ms foreground** debounce,
not a larger age bucket or a longer refresh policy. Timings include scheduler and
encoding overhead; the tests' 3s timeout is a failure watchdog, not a replacement
freshness interval. These observed timings are not a hard-real-time <=2000ms
wall-clock claim. As in #492, existing SSE clients age retained metadata locally;
new snapshots/initial SSE frames refresh its origin. No client protocol change.

### Paired real-fleet measurement (no causal CPU headline)

One invocation, both optimized binaries compiled BEFORE its first window:

```text
python3 docs/evidence/issue-560/measure.py /tmp/g560-base-target/release/corrald /tmp/g560-target/release/corrald
```

Raw exit **0**. Driver SHA-256:
`fa9cc19603fb63929e421eb156121cf8b46877c5c8fae0d9c3c44a3cc3431425`.
The same driver/process ran the before and after legs without modification.

Method: private loopback corrald instances with scratch HOME/config, reading the
same live herdr socket and local worktrees; one continuously connected SSE client;
15s warmup; scheduled **120s** window per leg; samples every 5s. CPU is the delta
of `ps time` (daemon user+system CPU, excluding child Git processes), divided by
measured monotonic wall time. Percent is one-core-equivalent average, not whole-
host CPU. Debug markers at actual snapshot-agent encoding and publication sites
count work in the window. Both builds enable the same marker logging. Base is the
pinned pre-fix source plus two diagnostic debug statements only; preparation and
source hashes are in `prepare-base.py` and `base-source.json`.

| Observation | Before | After |
|---|---:|---:|
| Observed live agents, min-max | 46-47 | 46-47 |
| Actual wall window, seconds | 120.108116792 | 120.043173666 |
| CPU counter start -> end, seconds | 16.57 -> 28.39 | 16.68 -> 24.68 |
| Daemon CPU consumed, seconds | 11.82 | 8.00 |
| Average daemon CPU, percent | 9.841133402 | 6.664268992 |
| Complete snapshot publications | 72 | 65 |
| Full agent-map encodes | 72 | 10 |
| Full agent-map encodes/minute | 35.967594159 | 4.998201744 |

Observed deltas: **-30.969392415 encodes/minute**, **-3.82 daemon CPU seconds**
over the respective 120s windows. The after leg made **55** metadata-only
publications without encoding the agent map. The remaining ten encodes are not
represented as unchanged-state ticks: this is a live, changing fleet, unlike the
controlled 200-agent regression.

This was not an invariant-membership replay or a stationary CPU microbenchmark:
initial roster hashes differ, membership varied transiently, and startup Git
work continued into the measured period. Host load also changed (before 1m load
5.87 -> 3.02; after 3.15 -> 4.17). Therefore the measured CPU difference is NOT
attributed entirely to serialization or advertised as a production CPU saving.
No rerun or hand-picked sub-window replaced this pair. All samples, roster hashes,
load observations, binary hashes, and raw encoder-log hashes are retained in
`measurements.json`. The raw private encoder logs were re-read and their counts
and SHA-256s verified against these records; both binary hashes still match.
Both temporary daemons and their SSE readers stopped. The owner's daemon was not
restarted, reconfigured or replaced.

## #560 publish-skip / encoder-reuse merge entry (2026-09-17)

Branch `g560-publishskip`, reviewed head `06dfac4937fc30f1ec5ab30a25c0053f1adc8897` (source `7696af2a`, evidence
commit docs-only), merged into `integration`. Receipt `4e6d185a` (the original correct fence stop) is preserved as an
ancestor.

The publish-skip guard never engaged: `flush()` early-returned only when `!pending_any && !git_plane_health.enabled &&
backlog unchanged`, and `enabled` is set once the coalescer is wired and never cleared, so every coalescer tick
re-serialized the FULL agent map — ~130 KB per publication at 200 agents, at 250 ms cadence with SSE subscribers
attached. The guard now compares real state:

    let same_agents = snapshot.agents == previous.agents.state;
    if !inner.pending_any && same_agents && previous.same_health(&snapshot) { return None; }
    let cached_agents = same_agents.then(|| previous.agents.clone());

Production diff: `src/core/store.rs` +16/-11, `src/core/store/published.rs` +101/-12, new
`src/core/store/publication_tests.rs` +335. No wire/schema change, no health-only SSE event, no invented revision,
no new dependency; `src/main.rs`, model/schema, HTTP/SSE handlers, adapters, manifests, `ios/`, `scripts/` and
`.github/` are byte-unchanged.

QUEUED-BLOCKER CONTEXT (orchestrator error, corrected in-loop): the lane first stopped report-only and CORRECTLY,
because the unconditional full-map serialization lives in the publication encoder
(`src/core/store/published.rs:29`, called from `store.rs:462`), which the original fence excluded. Amendment 1
authorized that file for the narrow byte-preserving purpose. The lane's own offered alternative - "accept periodic
full-map encoding" - was explicitly REJECTED: that deletes the defect instead of fixing it. It also refused to
relocate/duplicate the encoder, byte-splice published JSON from `store.rs`, or add a health-only SSE event.

RED, re-derived INDEPENDENTLY by the reviewer (not the implementer's artifact): base sources materialized at
`4e6d185` in a scratch worktree with only the new test extracted, contamination-checked (no `agent_encodings`, no
`EncodedAgents`, no `.agents.state`, no `publications()`) → **exit 101, 0 passed / 1 failed**, with the pre-fix
behaviour observed. The implementer's own RED at the pre-fix source also reports exit 101 with the assertion
"unchanged health-enabled ticks must not republish" and observed health OFF 0/10 vs health ON 10/10 republications.

GREEN and the behaviours that matter, observed at the reviewed head: `republications=0/10` with the health path BOTH
off and on (the defect is gone); **dead plane**: `republications=3/3` with `agent_encodings=0/3`, the encoded-agent
Arc identical across all three, and `age_ms` advancing 12013→14029→16044 while `agent_encodings=0` - i.e. ages keep
ticking on a stopped plane without re-encoding the map; `G560_CHANGE background_ms=2002`, `foreground_ms=253`
publication still happens on a real change within the documented bound; `G560_CACHED_BYTES states=64 snapshots=64
sse_frames=64 agent_encodings=0`; `G560_INVALIDATION upsert/noop/update/remove/reinsert/expiry/stop/recovery
verified`; and the #555a byte-equality oracle green (`G555_BYTE_EQUALITY states=64 snapshots=64 sse_frames=64
empty=1 legacy_header_cases=16 epochs=64 revisions…`).

ORCHESTRATOR BATTERY at the same head (mine, independent of the lane's drivers): focused guard test 0,
`randomized_publications_match_old_snapshot_and_sse_bytes` (the #555a oracle) 0, the encoder-bytes proof 0,
`cargo test --workspace` 0, `cargo fmt --all -- --check` 0, `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -D warnings` 0.
I also verified by diff that the final evidence commit touches ONLY `.report.md` and `docs/evidence/issue-560/**`
(zero source paths), so the reviewed source is exactly what merges.

INDEPENDENT REVIEW: PASS, no blocking findings, worktree pristine, head unchanged. It answered the orchestrator's
primary adversarial target ("can the comparison ever say unchanged when something changed?") as COVERAGE PROVEN with
a field-by-field comparison: `Agent` derives `PartialEq` over all 16 wire fields (`model.rs:173-200`); `same_health`
covers `git_plane_backlog`/`git_plane_alive`/… (`published.rs:81-105`); `generated_at` is deliberately excluded as
assembly time rather than state; no hidden state remains; the cached agent bytes necessarily correspond to the same
state the metadata describes (consumed inside one `PublishedSnapshot::new` call), so no state/metadata skew window;
the publication guard still spans capture -> blocking encode -> atomic replace before broadcast.
NON-BLOCKING FINDING 1: `same_health` compares by field list, so its coverage is total TODAY only because the wire
struct has exactly two fields - a future wire-visible field must be added there. Forward-looking, not live.

## #561 — preserve Git facts across normal supervisor generations

HEAD at entry creation (implementation, tests and evidence committed):
`16890e26cd0b51fe0f509a39aabc026924b2d1f5`. Branch `g561-hydrate`.
Base: `4cc316599b0ca1d69039aceb7dbb44193be48e52`.
Tested source HEAD: `f6ecffdbbc7ce8c36affda75bc6af7e54b146d40`;
production implementation commit: `83c6245bf14fb083754685944c926b38f16faed3`.
The only subsequent delivery change is this append-only report. Its containing
commit's SHA is supplied in the final message, not falsely self-embedded here.

### Implementation and acceptance map

Before: every generation set every cached branch/commit/subject/status and every
observation timestamp to None, and watcher startup debounced every known path.
After: complete observations and their exact ages survive a normal critical-child
exit or heartbeat-stall restart. New/unobserved paths still hydrate; removed
paths still leave the inventory. The real paced status safety net is retained.

Panic distinction: `src/adapters/git_plane.rs:786-819` gates the old complete
reset on `recover_after_panic`. Only `JoinError::is_panic()` sets it (:837),
including queued panic results discovered while aborting/joining siblings (:874).
A normal exit/cancellation is not a panic. All old children are joined before
replacement tasks start. Panic recovery clears ALL facts and ages, so watcher
bootstrap (:916-927) re-reads/re-emits both HeadMoved and DirtyChanged even for
otherwise unchanged worktrees. The real supervised-panic regression injects a
panic in the tracing writer after cache update but before send and requires both
fact events for both the interrupted and unaffected worktrees.

Normal cancellation can also interrupt publication: WorktreeState's
`publication_pending` (:489) is set before cache mutation in `apply_probe`
(:1569), forces complete replay on a subsequent equal probe, and clears only
after sends complete and the cached observation still matches (:1595-1604).
A separate blocked-channel/cancelled-publication test covers this boundary;
preserving the cache does not silently discard an interrupted publication.

### Behavioral RED / GREEN (raw subprocess exits)

SAME command on both sides:
`cargo test -p corrald g561_generation_preserves_facts -- --nocapture`.
RED was captured BEFORE production edits at the pinned base plus only the new
test include/harness. `red-head.txt` and lossless `red-source-diff.txt.gz` pin that
boundary. `red.json`: raw exit 101, 0 passed / 1 failed / 0 ignored.
The assertion is `generation restart cleared cached commit`, left `None`, right
`Some(<the temporary repository's real HEAD>)`; full raw output is `red.log.gz`.
The test takes all four Git permits before forcing a critical child to exit
normally, waits for generation 2, then checks cached commit/status/subject and
exact observation timestamp; eager refill cannot conceal a cleared cache.
GREEN: `final-green.json`, raw exit 0; 1 passed / 0 failed / 0 ignored
(18 test binaries/suites invoked, other suites filtered). It is the same test
and command, not a compile-only or zero-test green. Initial focused `g561_`
run: 5 passed / 0 failed / 1 ignored, exit 0; the ignored test is the cost witness,
which was separately executed on both sides, one passed test each, exit 0.

### Matched cost measurement and achieved bound

Command on BOTH sides:
`cargo test -p corrald g561_restart_cost_measurement -- --ignored --nocapture`.
A read-only filesystem census found 107 herdr worktree directories plus 23
Projects checkouts (130); this is not a live enabled-daemon inventory claim.
The repeatable fixture seeds 130 paths in ONE temporary repository (main plus
129 detached worktrees), fully warms facts, and forces three normal supervisor
generation changes. No live daemon or live repositories are restarted/modified.
Each window runs from before the restart request through 65s after generation
advance; wall time includes the one-second backoff. CPU is the delta of
getrusage SELF + reaped CHILDREN user/system seconds, including real Git children
and normal watcher/topology/sweep work, excluding setup/initial hydration.
The same debug harness, inventory, method and window were used on the same Mac.

Raw records (restart in integer ms; CPU/wall in seconds; percent of ONE core):

| side/trial | restart ms | CPU s | wall s | core % | Git counter | emitted | cumulative skipped |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| before/1 | 1015 | 21.189710 | 66.021472 | 32.095180 | 1005 | 260 | 44 |
| before/2 | 1012 | 38.998872 | 66.015815 | 59.075045 | 1281 | 260 | 44 |
| before/3 | 1005 | 36.600589 | 66.009084 | 55.447806 | 1409 | 260 | 44 |
| after/1 | 1014 | 12.671548 | 66.291344 | 19.114936 | 204 | 0 | 0 |
| after/2 | 1358 | 28.020863 | 66.371274 | 42.218359 | 504 | 0 | 5 |
| after/3 | 1012 | 35.538879 | 66.013386 | 53.835867 | 1003 | 0 | 6 |

Mean CPU/wall: before 32.263057 / 66.015457; after 25.410430 / 66.225335.
Observed mean CPU reduction 21.239856%; transition wall time did NOT improve.
After-run observed envelope: <=1358ms restart at the recorded millisecond
precision, <=35.538879 CPU seconds over <=66.371274 wall seconds. This is NOT a
host-independent CPU or deadline guarantee. The deterministic achieved bound is
ZERO eager watcher hydration probes for a fully observed unchanged inventory,
pinned by `g561_watcher_boot_has_zero_hydration_for_known_facts` (real watcher
bootstrap separated from the paced safety sweep). These measured values and the
zero-eager-probe bound are beside RESTART_DELAY at git_plane.rs:128-133;
freshness qualifications are beside SWEEP_INTERVAL at :118-125.

Limitations: shared host, no pre-run load readings; a mid-after-run load reading
was 73.32/49.09/38.50. Samples overlap/noise is large; no universal speedup claim.
A single repo does not reproduce 23 independent topology sources or large working
directories. `skipped` is cumulative including hydration, not a per-window delta.
Raw before/after logs and `measurements.json` preserve all six samples.

### Freshness and #492 health truthfulness

The changed-inventory test joins the old watcher while holding Git admission,
removes one linked path, adds another, changes the real main HEAD, and writes an
untracked file while the old watcher is stopped. It proves preserved old facts
before admission, then requires the changed HEAD AND dirty status, hydration of
the added path, removal of the deleted path, corresponding topology events, and
a strictly newer real observation timestamp. The initial ordinary focused run
used production cadence: convergence 585ms, alive=true, age_ms=5, skipped=1.

One gate failure is NOT hidden: the first coverage run exited 101 when this test
exceeded its 65s allowance (lib: 317 passed / 1 failed / 1 ignored). The failed
run lacked per-probe diagnostics; its exact cause is not proved and is not
conclusively blamed on host load. One successful 200ms-budget probe per first
round cannot be assumed; failed/over-budget work retains its old age and retries.
Only the test was corrected: existing SweepIntervals seam uses 1s status cadence
with the same production startup/topology timing, real Git, same restart and all
fact/event/health assertions. It pins the production 60s constant, allows 6s
including retries, and now prints facts/state/skipped on timeout. This is not a
6s production claim. Instrumented focused command:
`cargo llvm-cov --no-report -p corrald --lib -- g561_changed_inventory_and_missed_change_converge --nocapture`
exited 0, 1 passed, observed 1169ms / alive=true / age_ms=4 / skipped=1.
The earlier invalid cargo-llvm-cov filter spelling exited 1 without tests;
its receipt is retained, not reported as a behavioral failure or green.

Production freshness mechanism is unchanged: run_status_sweep (:1403-1466)
rescans and re-reads EVERY path, starting after 500ms, paced across the 60s
interval with four Git command permits. Scan/admission latency is additional;
errors/over-budget results retry rather than pretending stale facts are fresh.
Watcher metadata changes still debounce promptly. Preservation is not a skip of
future observations. Observation ages are only advanced by successful probes,
not generation changes. The test exercises real HTTP snapshot health output,
checks alive=true while running / false after shutdown, numeric last-event age,
and an exactly incremented skipped counter after a deliberate over-budget probe.
The separate existing #492 suite passed all 7 tests (final-g492, exit 0).

### Converted and deliberately retained call sites

- Converted supervise :786 and panic-result handling :837/:874; normal restart
  preserves facts, panic recovery retains the complete reset.
- Converted watcher bootstrap :916-927; only missing/interrupted observations
  debounce, not all known paths.
- Converted shared apply_probe :1476/:1547-1604; publication replay applies to
  BOTH callers, debounce :1293 and paced sweep :1458, without changing budgets.
- Kept watcher event debounce :964; changed metadata still needs prompt refresh.

- Kept startup/topology/rediscovery rescans :1343/:1370/:1389 and added-path
  debounces :1349/:1376/:1395; discover new paths, drop removed paths, preserve
  failed-source backoff.
- Kept status-sweep rescan :1421 and probe :1446; the anti-staleness backstop.
- Kept Plane::start :2529; still exactly one supervisor. No store/wire changes.

## #561 git-plane fact preservation merge entry (2026-09-17)

Branch `g561-hydrate`, reviewed head `200b6c2b1e5c9162733f93800eddcac9682f775e` (tested source
`f6ecffdb`, later commits evidence/report only — proven by diff), merged into `integration`.

A supervisor generation change over an UNCHANGED worktree inventory no longer wipes every cached git fact. The
wholesale reset is now gated on `recover_after_panic` (`git_plane.rs:788`, used `:799`/`:808`, cleared `:819`), which
is set ONLY by `JoinError::is_panic()` (`:837`, and the abort/join drain `:874`). A normal critical-child exit, a
heartbeat stall, an `abort_all()` cancellation and a sink close all leave facts and ages intact; a genuine panic
still clears facts AND ages so the watcher bootstrap re-emits HeadMoved and DirtyChanged for otherwise-unchanged
worktrees. `src/adapters/git_plane.rs` +88/-22 plus a new `git_plane_issue561_tests.rs` +474.

INDEPENDENT REVIEW: PASS, no blocking findings, head unchanged, worktree pristine. The review was thorough in the
way that matters here:
- It re-derived the RED at base with the COMMITTED harness (exit 101, panic at
  `git_plane_issue561_tests.rs:55:5`, "generation restart cleared cached commit", `left None` vs the temp repo's real
  HEAD) - the same assertion the lane's own red.log shows.
- It MUTATED the panic wiring: making a normal critical-child exit set `recover_after_panic` (splice into the `_` arm
  at `:843`) produced exit 1 with that same assertion, proving the flag is load-bearing. It also reported honestly
  that its FIRST mutation attempt (flag initialised true at `:788`) was INERT because `:819` resets the flag before
  the observed generation, and replaced it - a mutation that cannot fail proves nothing, and the reviewer said so.
- It red-probed the zero-hydration bound test at base sources: exit 101, FAILED at
  `git_plane_issue561_tests.rs:333:5`, `left 1, right 0` - the base made exactly one eager hydration probe - while at
  head the same test observes 0. The bound discriminates in BOTH directions and the test genuinely isolates watcher
  bootstrap (it spawns `run_watcher` directly, and the counter increments in the single probe entry point
  `probe_worktree_admitted`), so the code comment's "zero eager probes is the pinned bound" does not overclaim.
- Panic-path matrix (all read and reasoned, not asserted): normal exit -> `_` arm `:841-844`, no flag; real panic ->
  `:837`; cancellation via `abort_all()` `:864-867` -> cancelled JoinErrors, no flag; panic discovered while
  draining -> `:874` `|=`; stall -> `:848-858`, no flag; sink close -> `:832`/`:877-879`, no restart. Ordering: every
  break falls into `abort_all` then a FULL drain before `RESTART_DELAY` and before the next generation spawns, so no
  old-generation probe can publish after replacement starts.
- Measured cost re-derived arithmetically from the committed artifacts: mean CPU 32.263057 s -> 25.410430 s, a
  21.2399% reduction, with `emitted` 260 -> 0 structurally consistent with the mechanism.

ORCHESTRATOR BATTERY at the same head: focused `g561_` tests 0, `cargo test --workspace` 0, `cargo fmt --all --
--check` 0, `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` 0, `cargo deny check` 0 (advisories ok, bans
ok, licenses ok, sources ok), `cargo audit` 0. The evidence commits were proven docs-only by diff, and the head was
unchanged after the battery.

FINDINGS CARRIED FORWARD (non-blocking, none verdict-changing):
1. RED-evidence provenance gap: the committed harness does not compile against base sources as-is (one sibling test
   reads `WorktreeState.publication_pending`, a field this fix introduces), so the committed RED artifacts came from
   an earlier harness draft; the reviewer reproduced the RED with the committed harness, so the CLAIM holds.
   Recommendation for future lanes: commit the test FIRST, then the fix, so RED-time harness bytes are pinnable.
2. **Independence, flagged by the reviewer for owner adjudication:** the implementer lane AND this reviewer BOTH
   served on the configured fallback model (`glm-5.3-flash`/zai), verified in the profile session DB; the profile
   default (`claude-opus-5`/anthropic) was not in effect for either. Fresh-context independence holds; model
   diversity does not.
3. Timing-sensitive fixtures: the changed-inventory phase times real git operations against a 6 s allowance (the
   lane's own first-run 65 s timeout was disclosed and fixed via the seam rather than by loosening it). Noted.
Owner gates: simulator/CI claims only where run; no live daemon or live repository was restarted or modified.

# Issue #562 — binding lookup matches aliased worktree spellings

Worktree: `/Users/jirathip/.herdr/worktrees/corral/impl562-pathmatch`.
Branch: `g562-pathmatch`. Base: `79781ead3336550e6f792e354df875f7d42f3715` (current `integration`,
the #560 merge). The preceding #560 entry above is preserved byte-for-byte as this file's prefix.

Delivered head (source + evidence commit, message "fix: match aliased worktree paths in snapshot bindings (#562)"): `01a118eedc0af4dc9a9c1a1fab4f0a0913ab37a7`. Its tree differs from the final amended commit — which carries this report entry — by exactly this paragraph: the report cannot name its own commit's SHA. Sources are pinned by the `source_sha256` hashes in `docs/evidence/issue-562/summary.json`. Files changed (lines added/removed):

```
 src/core/store.rs                   | 17 +  5 -   (fix)
 src/core/store/publication_tests.rs | 169 + 0 -   (regression: fixture helpers, alias test, canonical fixtures)
 docs/evidence/issue-562/compare.py  | 43 +  0 -    (base-vs-fixed wire comparison driver)
 docs/evidence/issue-562/**          | evidence (logs, summary.json, run-gates.py)
```

## The lookup, before and after

Before (`src/core/store.rs:631` at base — exact `HashMap`/`BTreeMap` string key):

```rust
let fresh = ws
    .worktree_path
    .as_ref()
    .and_then(|path| ages.get(path))
    .is_some_and(|age| !age.stale);
```

After:

```rust
let fresh = ws
    .worktree_path
    .as_ref()
    .and_then(|path| worktree_age(&ages, path))
    .is_some_and(|age| !age.stale);
```

```rust
// Keep canonical-path hits cheap; only aliases/missing facts scan the keys.
// Both fallback insertion and binding projection must use the same identity.
fn worktree_age<'a>(ages: &'a BTreeMap<String, GitFactAge>, path: &str) -> Option<&'a GitFactAge> {
    ages.get(path).or_else(|| {
        ages.iter()
            .find(|(key, _)| paths_match(Path::new(key), Path::new(path)))
            .map(|(_, age)| age)
    })
}
```

The same helper replaces the insertion guard at what is now line 618, so a
non-canonical spelling can no longer manufacture its own `stale: true` entry either (that
duplicate key is what the #556 fixture documented). `paths_match` (`src/core/workspace.rs:248`) is
called unmodified — the raw-equality fast path stays first, exactly as at the other call sites.

## RED — raw exit, at the pre-fix head

```
cargo test -p corrald g562_ -- --nocapture     # production source unmodified at base
exit 101
test result: FAILED. 1 passed; 1 failed; 0 ignored; 319 filtered out
```

Failing assertion, quoted verbatim (`/tmp/g562-red3.log`, gzipped at
`docs/evidence/issue-562/red3.log.gz`):

```
thread 'core::store::publication_tests::g562_aliased_worktree_binding_tracks_fact_freshness' panicked
  at src/core/store/publication_tests.rs:744:
assertion `left == right` failed: fresh: fresh aliased worktree must retain its PR binding
    (/private/var/folders/4k/fbv06j2j5bbd3h4wgbwk1ccm0000gn/T/.tmpbkRRKl/a-link/worktree)
  left: None
 right: Some(556)
```

The pre-fix snapshot proves the mechanism: the aliased agent gets `pr_number: null` and its own
synthetic key `/…/a-link/worktree -> { fact_age_ms: null, stale: true }` while the canonical
peer's `pr_number: 556` survives on the real fact key (verbatim wire bytes in
`docs/evidence/issue-562/base-alias-fresh.json`). As the issue says, this is fail-closed — no
stale data is rendered — so the effect is the hidden binding, not a wrong one.

`g560_dead_plane_ages_reuse_agent_encoding`, `g560_agent_cache_invalidates_on_content_and_stale_projection`,
`g560_cached_encoder_matches_independent_bytes`, `g560_equal_age_still_refreshes_its_time_origin`)
stays green — #560's guard logic is untouched by this diff (no line outside
`snapshot_locked`'s two call sites and the new helper).

The alias test proves the fix is discriminating, not blanket-fresh: for each of four spellings
(`a-link/worktree` and `z-link/worktree` symlinked prefixes, `repo/worktree/../worktree`, and
`repo//worktree/` with a duplicate slash and trailing slash — each asserted to differ from the
canonical string, canonicalise to it, and `paths_match` it) it walks the freshness matrix:
`fresh -> expired(121s) -> recovered -> stopped -> absent -> fresh-again`. The binding is present
exactly in the fresh phases, suppressed in expired/stopped/absent, and the alias never appears as
its own `git_worktree_facts` key — so canonical-case semantics and the stale flags are unchanged,
only the identity used to find the fact is. 24 phase transitions, 2 agents each; GREEN prints
`G562_ALIAS_VERIFIED spellings=4 transitions=24 agents_per_transition=2`.

## Canonical-case byte identity (AC3)

`g562_canonical_snapshot_fixtures` seeds the two #556 fixture agents under controlled health
states and prints the actual published `/snapshot` wire bytes for `fresh`, `stopped`,
`unobserved` and `absent`. `docs/evidence/issue-562/compare.py` re-extracts those bytes from the
RED and GREEN logs and compares them byte-for-byte after masking only `epoch` and
`generated_at` (the two fields AC3 exempts):

```

{"case": "absent", "bytes": 1466, "sha256": "4ba763c716efa92d567613366b7301f3b32e004508c85e4466c31ab67f962cc1", "byte_identical": true}
{"case": "fresh", "bytes": 1560, "sha256": "0f3fd69ba40b8c2f9173ccdc5967297bda5a4bd1fc8d29d5e2272b3fc9bad37c", "byte_identical": true}
{"case": "stopped", "bytes": 1461, "sha256": "173923e13b03e32632e9f655458910b69b8f8369ec17b4ec129d6e8b10b90e06", "byte_identical": true}
{"case": "unobserved", "bytes": 1466, "sha256": "4ba763c716efa92d567613366b7301f3b32e004508c85e4466c31ab67f962cc1", "byte_identical": true}
PASS: 4 canonical snapshots; only epoch/generated_at masked
```

The per-case JSON files are committed next to the driver
(`docs/evidence/issue-562/{base,fixed}-canonical-*.json`). Note `unobserved` == `absent`: a
canonical path with no fact legitimately gains a synthetic `stale: true` key at BOTH heads — that
insertion is not the bug and is unchanged. `fresh`/`stopped` reuse the same committed
`docs/evidence/issue-556/rust/fixture-fresh.json` shape the issue points at, via the committed
issue-556 precedent: same keys, same fields, same masking set. The #556 evidence directory itself
is byte-unchanged (`git diff --exit-code <base> -- docs/evidence/issue-556` → 0), and the fixture
is consumed read-only through `include_str!` rather than copied, so no new fixture file exists to
drift.

## Call-site inventory (AC4)

Converted (this diff):

- `src/core/store.rs:633` the binding/age lookup — the bug. Now `worktree_age(&ages, path)`.
- `src/core/store.rs:618` the same map's synthetic-entry insertion — same helper, because
  inserting under the raw spelling is what manufactured the duplicate stale key in the first
  place. They are the only two places in the crate that key `ages` by an agent path.

Deliberately left, with reasons:

- `src/integrate/mod.rs:368,431,456` — already `paths_match`; the sibling pattern this fix
  converges on.
- `src/adapters/herdr.rs:1948` — already `paths_match` (repo-match detection).
- `src/adapters/git_plane.rs` — all path state (`worktrees`, `by_branch`, `source_worktrees`,
  `source_failures`, `main_checkouts`) is keyed by `canonicalize_existing_prefix` at ingestion
  (lines 604, 649, 759, 1069, 3279). Exact `get()` there is canonical-by-construction; adding
  `paths_match` would re-canonicalise already-canonical keys on a periodic sweep. This is the
  plane that defines the canonical spelling, so it is the wrong side to soften.
- `src/adapters/git_plane.rs:1039,1672,1749` — `watchers`/`worktrees` keyed by `commondirs` and
  by the `tracked` map's own keys; both sides are canonical path sets produced by that same
  ingestion (verified at those lines), never a raw herdr cwd. Exact `contains_key`/`get` is the
  correct identity there.
- `src/core/workspace.rs:111,159,182,188,206` — same reason: `roots`/`worktree_aliases` are
  inserted canonicalised and `facts_for` canonicalises the query first. Out of fence regardless.
- `src/core/store/published.rs:95-104` (`same_health`) — compares `git_worktree_facts` maps that
  are built by this same snapshot path; with the insertion fixed, the two sides now share one key
  set, so it needs no comparison change. Left byte-identical (and it is the #560 guard's field
  list — not mine to change).
- `ios/**` — the client renders whatever `worktree_path` the daemon publishes (raw, by contract);
  it never resolves the path against a fact map. Out of fence.
- `scripts/**`, `.github/**` — no path-fact matching.

## Cost / bound of the lookup

`BTreeMap::get` on the exact key first, which is the production steady state (the git plane and
herdr agree on the spelling — the reviewer measured 25/25 and 23/23 canonical paths on both live
hosts), so the hot path stays an allocation-free O(log n) probe as before. Only a miss — an
aliased spelling or a genuinely unknown path — falls back to a linear scan over the map keys;
`paths_match` itself short-circuits on raw equality first and only calls
`canonicalize_existing_prefix`, which walks existing parent components (no `fs::canonicalize`).

Measured current population, from this host right now:

```
herdr agent list  ->  exit 0, 50 agents, 0 aliased cwds
```

`docs/evidence/issue-562/agent-count.json` records the raw command and result. So the scan bound
is 50 keys × ~5 components per miss, and misses are per-agent-path, once per snapshot build — not
per tick per key. Even at 10x the fleet the fallback is a few thousand component comparisons
inside a projection the coalescer already runs on a 250 ms/2 s cadence, which is why a scan was
chosen over a wider wire change: changing `git_worktree_facts` keys (e.g. de-duplicating
aliases) would touch the published contract and #560's publication-identity comparison, both
explicitly out of bounds here.

## #562 aliased worktree path merge entry (2026-09-17)

Branch `g562-pathmatch`, reviewed head `d36df9024a3609812fafdcda6f9c0e3f6ad9fe8c`, merged into `integration`.
`src/core/store.rs` +22/-5 plus tests in `src/core/store/publication_tests.rs` +169.

The snapshot's binding/age lookup resolved a worktree path by EXACT string (`ages.get(path)`), while the health/fact
keys are canonicalised by the git plane - so an aliased spelling produced a synthetic `stale: true` entry and the
PR/CI binding was silently hidden. It now goes through the shared helper:

    fn worktree_age<'a>(ages: &'a BTreeMap<String, GitFactAge>, path: &str) -> Option<&'a GitFactAge> {
        ages.get(path).or_else(|| ages.iter()
            .find(|(key, _)| paths_match(Path::new(key), Path::new(path))).map(|(_, age)| age))
    }

used at both binding sites (`store.rs:618` guarding the synthetic insert, `:633` the freshness check). No ad-hoc
normalisation; `paths_match` (workspace.rs) is REUSED, not modified - and workspace.rs, the other adapters, `ios/`,
`scripts/` and `.github/**` are byte-unchanged.

INDEPENDENT REVIEW: PASS, no blocking findings, head unchanged, worktree pristine. The reviewer attacked the two
things that could have made this pass vacuously:
- ALIAS VALIDITY: the fixture uses FOUR genuine spellings (two symlinked prefixes, a `..` form, and a `//`+trailing
  slash form), each explicitly asserted to differ from its canonical form - so the test cannot pass on a spelling
  that canonicalises identically.
- BYTE IDENTITY, masking honesty: it ran the committed `compare.py` over ITS OWN base/head logs - exit 0, 4 cases
  (fresh/stopped/unobserved/absent), the masked set exactly `[epoch, generated_at]`, and the four case sha256s
  byte-identical to the committed outputs. A comparison masking more than those two fields would have satisfied the
  AC on paper while hiding a change; it does not.
- MUTATION: it replaced `worktree_age`'s body with a bare `ages.get(path)` (fallback removed) - raw exit 101, same
  witness line (`publication_tests.rs:744`, `left: None, right: Some(556)`), ZERO collateral (the canonical test
  stayed green), restore verified by sha256 equal to the summary.json pin, then control exit 0 / 2 passed.
- BASE RED re-derived independently: scratch worktree at `79781ea` with only the head's test file checked out
  (`git diff --exit-code src/core/store.rs` = 0 confirmed BEFORE the run) - exit 101, 1 passed / 1 failed, the
  aliased agent's fresh binding suppressed at base, matching the lane's own red3.log.gz.
- CROSS-LANE INTERACTION WITH #560 (an orchestrator-named target): "none harmful" - #560's guard is
  `store.rs:398-399`, whose single production caller `same_health` (`published.rs:95-112`) compares the facts maps by
  length plus positional fields, so the canonicalisation does not make #560's unchanged-state comparison see a
  phantom difference or miss a real one.
- The lane also ran the full gate set itself (workspace 0 / 279.8 s, clippy 0, deny 0, audit 0, release 0, coverage 0)
  and pinned `source_sha256` for the two changed sources.

ORCHESTRATOR BATTERY at the same head: pinned hashes verified ON DISK (store.rs `efe754fe3a72…`,
publication_tests.rs `32fbd0839685…` - matching the pins), scope confirmed to exactly those two `src/` files, focused
`g562_` tests 0 with `G562_ALIAS_VERIFIED spellings=4 transitions=24 agents_per_transition=2`, `cargo test
--workspace` 0, fmt 0, clippy 0, `cargo deny check` 0. The battery built under `/tmp` and removed its target, and the
head was unchanged after the run.

DISCLOSURE: this review was served by the configured fallback model (`glm-5.3-flash`/zai) after account-level
anthropic rate limiting - disclosed by the reviewer and confirmed by me from the profile log for session
`20260917_115025_9b4fda`. **The implementer lane for this issue ALSO ran on the profile fallback
(`deepseek-v4-flash`)** - its primary failed mid-lane - so the gate has fresh-context independence but the pair is
not model-diverse. Recorded for owner visibility rather than smoothed over.

  harness. Matches for XCUITest in historical evidence prose concern Dynamic Type launch arguments,
  not an executable UI test. The native project inspection below independently confirms no UI target.

Minimal authorization needed to continue: permit a dedicated XCUITest target in `ios/project.yml`,
its generated project/scheme changes, and the UI-test source path (or name an existing approved

### 2. Candidate rules — written, NOT runtime-validated

`HerdEdgeGeometry` uses the measured group and unobscured viewport in global coordinates.
The group includes the full button/caption and the current painted art bounds (path bounds,
strokes and bob offset). In the normal layout, with clearance equal to the minimum of the
upper and lower group-to-viewport gaps, the candidate opacity is:

    clamp((clearance - 1) / 7, 0, 1)

Thus its mathematical boundary is zero at one point of clearance, before crossing either edge;
full opacity needs eight points. An invalid viewport yields zero. The complete rendered button
is composited and faded as a unit, not gradient-masked. The painted button's hit testing is
conditional on opacity being positive. Its semantic representation remains separate.
The landscape, rail, repository chip and navigation are outside this group modifier.

The normal paddock candidate uses measured eager row frames, with column count derived from
available width and the existing minimum column width/spacing. `HerdRowSnap` changes the native
scroll target to the nearest clamped measured row origin, including start and end candidates.
Each row includes its eight-point top inset. Bottom space is derived from the viewport and
measured last-row height so that the last row can settle complete. There is no programmatic
content-offset gesture driver or repeated correction loop in the production snap path.

The large-accessibility path retains free column scrolling. If a group's height plus the
sixteen-point fit margin exceeds the viewport, the candidate leaves it opaque/readable rather
than permanently hiding it. This is the proposed exception to the full-group-fit rule, NOT yet
a tested exception. Accessibility focus is intended to reveal the focused row via the scroll
reader. No explicit animated snap is introduced for Reduce Motion. These accessibility and
motion claims remain design intent until their tests actually run.

- The runner consumes the measurements as an accessibility VALUE on a DEBUG-only, launch-flag-gated element:
  `HerdView.swift:224-225` (`.accessibilityIdentifier("g568-geometry")` +
  `.accessibilityValue(HerdEdgeEvidence.value(...))`), inside the `#if DEBUG` block `HerdView.swift:199-233`;
  the encoder is `HerdView.swift:901-914`. The XCUITest reads `app.otherElements["g568-geometry"].value`
  (`ios/FleetNotifierUITests/HerdEdgeGestureTests.swift:36-48`). Why it cannot bypass a gate: it is read-only
  app state exposed to the accessibility tree under `-corral568EdgeEvidence` only, it writes no files, it is not
  compiled into Release, and no repository gate reads it.

### 3. The two rules (measured, not hardcoded)

- Fade rule: `HerdEdgeGeometry.opacity(group:viewport:allowsOversized:)` (`HerdView.swift:612-617`) computes
  `clearance = min(group.minY - viewport.minY, viewport.maxY - group.maxY)` and returns
  `clamp((clearance - 1) / (8 - 1))` — a 1 pt guard band, full opacity at the 8 pt row inset. Zero opacity is
  reached while the group still has >= 1 pt inside the viewport, i.e. before any part of the group crosses the
  boundary. Art and caption are one group painted with `.transaction{$0.animation=nil}`; no mask.
- Snap rule: `HerdEdgeGeometry.snap(proposed:rows:contentHeight:viewportHeight:)` (`HerdView.swift:622-627`)
  clamps the proposed offset to `[0, contentHeight - viewportHeight]` and returns the nearest candidate among
  `{0, end}` plus the MEASURED row minYs (preference `HerdRowFrames`), which makes a settled offset idempotent.
- Live-remeasure settlement (`HerdView.swift:705-711, 726, 754, 766-784`): when a live caption reflow changes a
  row's height after deceleration has ended, the scroll waits 150 ms of measured-offset quiescence (skipping
  while the finger is down or while the page is offscreen) and re-anchors the nearest measured row with
  `reader.scrollTo(row.key, anchor:.top)` without animation.

tap, the semantic row survives and is tappable after a real drag reveals it. `day-blocked-accessibility`: Reduce

run. `day-single`/`day-no-field`: single-row and empty paddocks.

scrolling — every recorded case reports `duringScroll=true` and `overlapsDrag=true` (`G568_LIVE_UPDATES` lines).

Result: no horse or caption is vertically sliced at either edge in any inspected frame; settled rows are complete
with captions; night palette and chrome intact; no stray overlay or stray geometry visible.

### 7. Accessibility

- Oversized fallback: `allowsOversized` renders a group that cannot fit (`group.height + 2*inset >
  viewport.height`) at opacity 1 instead of fading it. Documented at `HerdView.swift:614`, tested
  deterministically (`testEdgeOversizedAccessibilityFallbackKeepsGroupReadable`) and exercised in the UI runner
  by `day-blocked-accessibility` (Reduce Motion + AccessibilityXXXL), which asserts a fully opaque oversized
  sample (`samples.values.contains { $0.oversized && $0.opacity == 1 }`).
- Touch exclusion: a real tap on a fully transparent group's overlap area did not present the selection sheet,
  while the semantic button survived and an ordinary tap after a real drag revealed it:
  `G568_TAP_PASS hidden=::edge-02 semanticReachable=true` (case `day-empty-taps`).
- Focus: the faded group keeps its AX identity and label; the runtime focus-change reveal is exercised
  deterministically (`testEdgeFocusChangeRevealsPreviouslyFadedRowWithoutAnimation` proves a focused, previously
  faded row is revealed by the measured settle; the fixture's observed content offset is asserted non-zero).
- NOT verified: live VoiceOver gesture delivery (swiping through a fade with VoiceOver speaking). simctl cannot
  inject VoiceOver gestures and no injector may be installed on this host, so that remains the owner's device gate.

### 8. Pin, both directions

- Recomputed with the repository's own function (`release_source_digest`), never hand-edited.
- OLD pin `b4f8a24d91e1b267ad7a5a77767a0acc419539a05fa1033a16453d62b7f7c785`; NEW pin
  `e81e0a5c308efaf6ecee4d208eb4c063bd901d0a3f45a0a931eb69ebcc6b340b`.
- Direction 1: the current tree's digest equals the new pin (`current_release_digest == current_pins`).
- Direction 2: a `git archive` of base `4cc3165` recomputes the OLD pin exactly
  (`archived_base_release_digest == old_pins`), proving no other covered file's bytes were absorbed.
- The separate `APPROVED_TEST_SOURCE_DIGEST` did NOT move: `5c27ace68bd25d7ab958303ccac5f38a1518b7cc4877072b8e764bca8d1b4da8`
  in base and at head, because it is a plain sha256 of `ios/FleetNotifierTests/FleetNotifierTests.swift`
  (`check-release-demo.py:41,1452`), which is untouched; the new tests live in the unpinned `HerdTests.swift`.
- The `check-release-demo.py` diff vs base is exactly the constant plus three comment lines.
- Evidence: `docs/evidence/issue-568/amend-2-pin-proof.log` and the provenance log
  `docs/evidence/issue-568/logs/amend-2-provenance-driver.log`.

### 9. Hygiene, append-only, status

- `git diff --check` raw exit 0 at head d6a533a (gate row above).
- Append-only proof: `provenance.py` asserts the current `.report.md` starts with the byte prefix of
  `.report.md` at receipt `0c3d6a7` (263718 bytes, 3806 lines, sha256
  `639be8c2afba3f044bb75da62ab1e79256be744972f2786a7be0db5c26dad7d0`) — this amendment only appends.
- XcodeGen: regenerated with `xcodegen generate`; the project and scheme are byte-identical on a second
  generation, and the only change is the ADDED UI-test target, its scheme wiring and its sources (11 added
  objects; pre-existing objects unchanged except the new group/target references). The UI-test target does not
  ship, is not in the app product, and is not a substitute for the physical-device gate.

### 10. What I could NOT verify (disclosed, not smoothed)

- Gesture fidelity: XCUITest input is synthetic. It reproduces drag/flick/reversal/overscroll with real timing
  and velocity, but I cannot prove it is kinematically identical to a human drag; every video is labelled
  simulator-only and the physical-iPhone feel remains the OWNER's gate.
- Frame alignment: simctl's media clock and XCTest's wall clock are not guaranteed to agree, so each extracted
  frame carries the +/-1 s caveat recorded in the manifests; the edge-crossing check is therefore visual at the
  named phases, not a millisecond-exact claim.
- Live VoiceOver gesture delivery (section 7) and any hosted-CI claim are out of scope here; no CI ran for this
  head, and no PR/branch push happened.
- Two intermediate battery runs exist in this lane's history: one aborted by a stale output directory, one
  UI-run flake caused by my own over-strict live-update overlap assertion (an earlier RED at head 78a6d2a failed
  `night-empty` on "a live update must overlap a real drag action"). The flake was fixed at the source of the
  assertion (the runner's gesture schedule is not clock-aligned with the fixture's cadence) and the final
  battery above is the result; the stale logs are archived at /tmp/g568-a2-archive-run3/ and /tmp/g568-a2-archive-pre-final/.
- The recorded clips are in /tmp (paths + sha256 above); if the owner wants them in-repo, they need an explicit
  size decision (about 600 MB) — I did not commit them.

## #568 Herd scroll-edge snapping + crossfades merge entry (2026-09-17)

Branch `g568-herd-edge`, reviewed head `3b9b387ed17a962e41ae968f472b11e9d6e5c340`, merged into `integration`.
Complete-row snapping plus whole-group crossfades at the visible scroll edges, keeping the scrollable paddock (no
fixed scene, no new vertical pagination).

**The fix.** `HerdEdgeGeometry.opacity(group:viewport:allowsOversized:)` and
`...snap(proposed:rows:contentHeight:viewportHeight:)` in `ios/FleetNotifier/UI/Herd/HerdView.swift`; a single
`compositingGroup().opacity(...)` on painted content (no `.mask(`), `.allowsHitTesting(opacity > 0)`, semantic content
kept independent of painted opacity for VoiceOver, and native `scrollTargetBehavior` settling rather than a timer
fighting momentum. Fade rule: `clearance = min(group.minY - viewport.minY, viewport.maxY - group.maxY)`,
`opacity = clamp((clearance - 1) / 7, 0, 1)` - zero BEFORE any ink crosses, symmetric top and bottom.

**Evidence.** 10 committed gesture clips (6.2 MB total, sized to the repo's 0.2-1.4 MB/clip precedent) covering
short+tall viewports, Day+Night, blocked+empty rail, accessibility and tap variants; 78 phase-anchored frames;
deterministic opacity-boundary and row/end-snap tests against MEASURED geometry; the RED witness re-derived
independently by the reviewer via a byte-identical mutation (`opacity := 1.0`, sha256 `1171a49e5d49...` == the lane's
recorded `mutation_sha256`) producing exit 65 with 90 real assertion failures; the release-source pin proven in both
directions (head recompute == new pin, base recompute == the old `b4f8a24d...`); xcodegen drift 0 lines.

**Additive-only project change**: a new XCUITest target (`ios/FleetNotifierUITests`), `project.yml` + the
xcodegen-regenerated `project.pbxproj`/`xcscheme`; 0 removed lines in the pbxproj and only FleetNotifierUITests-scoped
additions; app target, existing unit-test target, deployment targets, signing and entitlements unchanged. This was
authorized by orchestrator amendment after the lane correctly refused to substitute `setContentOffset` for real
gestures or to install a host gesture tool.

**THE VERIFICATION HOLD (and why the merge is still correct).** My own battery's `full` leg FAILED: unit suite green
(665 tests / 1 skipped / 0 failures), but the gesture UI suite reported `7 tests, with 1 failure` -
`testDayEmptyRailEdges`, assertion `HerdEdgeGestureTests.swift:120` "the fixture's live updates must land while the
runner is scrolling the field". The preceding `updates.count == 4` assertion passed, so the fixture delivered its
updates; the wall-clock overlap missed. The test's own comment concedes the timing assumption ("not clock-aligned"),
and the app logged repeated `[stream] stream connection error: host unreachable`. A first re-run attempt was INVALID
and discarded (`hermes-sim-task: simulator did not reach booted state`, host load 404, disk 1.6 GiB). A bounded
observer then waited for a quiet host and re-ran the SAME head: **load 6, `Executed 7 tests, with 0 failures`,
`** TEST SUCCEEDED **`, with `testDayEmptyRailEdges` PASSING (92.6 s)**. Conclusion: load-induced flake, not a defect.

**Non-blocking finding recorded:** that assertion guards an owner-visible "live updates during scrolling" claim while
depending on wall-clock overlap between the fixture's updates and the runner's gesture window, so it is
load-sensitive by construction. It is worth hardening in a follow-up; it is NOT a defect in the snapping fix, and the
deterministic tests covering the same property are timing-independent.

**Disclosures.** Review served by the configured fallback `glm-5.3-flash` after account-level anthropic rate limiting
(verified from the profile log for session `20260917_152201_f41c8f`); the implementer lane ran on `deepseek-v4-flash`
(lane status showed a 3h50m session). Review and implementation were therefore fresh-context independent but not
model-diverse. The lane also honestly labels its gesture input as synthetic XCUITest input with real timing and
velocity but unproven kinematic identity to a human drag, simulator-only, with physical-device feel explicitly the
owner's gate.
