# Issue #563 — two-sided hashed-ref identity (owner-approved OPTION A)

Lane: implementer · Worktree: `/Users/jirathip/.herdr/worktrees/corral/impl563-hashedid`
Branch: `g563-hashedid` · BASE `e0fe2e6c46d8dc3ecec36680850c29dd6778323e` (current `integration`, verified on disk).
Implementation commit: `c6cda7f88f564734a92c21c841cea03c0b93d968`
(`fix: hash-equality ref identity so redaction never costs a binding (#563)`).
This append plus the evidence logs is the delivery commit on top of it; the `src/`/`tests/`
bytes are identical in both (see "Gates"). The two prior worktree copies of this file
(the #547/#548 merge record and every later section) are preserved byte-for-byte as the
prefix above.

## FENCE (files touched)

`git diff --numstat e0fe2e6..c6cda7f -- src tests`:

```
139  8   src/adapters/gh_plane.rs
54   18  src/integrate/mod.rs
278  1   tests/gh_plane.rs
138  0   tests/integration.rs
```

Plus `docs/evidence/issue-563/**` (driver, pin scan, 10 raw logs, `redgreen.json`).
No other file was touched: no `ios/`, no `scripts/`, no `.github/`, no other `src/` file,
no `Cargo.toml`/`Cargo.lock`/`deny.toml`.

## IDENTITY PARTICIPANTS (where the local branch/commit participates in matching, and how each compares now)

The wire side (`src/adapters/gh_plane.rs`):

| file:line | role | now |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `gh_plane.rs:812` | `ref_identity(raw)` — ordinary value passes through byte-unchanged; credential-shaped value → `[REDACTED:sha256:<64-hex>]` | new |
| `gh_plane.rs:825` | `ref_identity_placeholder(raw)` — unconditional digest placeholder (active-token taint path) | new |
| `gh_plane.rs:833-865` | `redact_response`: `headRefOid`/`headRefName` are **no longer exempt** — value projected via `ref_identity`, or via the placeholder when the raw value carries the active token | replaced the old carve-out |

The local side (`src/integrate/mod.rs`) — every place the local branch reaches identity or the read model:

| file:line | role | now |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `integrate/mod.rs:245` (`HeadMoved`) | records the branch into the SHARED attribution (the value the convergence fallback and the Herdr adapter copy onto records) | `ref_identity(&branch)` |
| `integrate/mod.rs:271` (`CommitOnBranch`) | same recording | `ref_identity(&branch)` |
| `integrate/mod.rs:355` (`converge` attribution fallback) | copies the attribution fact onto matching records | inherits the projection (fact is already the redacted identity) |
| `integrate/mod.rs:415` (`reapply_path`) | snapshot/SSE egress of the local branch from the raw git-facts cache | `ref_identity(branch)` |
| `integrate/mod.rs:429` (`reapply_path`) | snapshot/SSE egress of the local head commit | `facts.commit.as_deref().map(ref_identity)` (byte-unchanged for a real oid) |
| `integrate/mod.rs:552-553` + filters `:558`/`:565` (`apply_pr_facts`) | **the comparison** — head-SHA match and the (repo, branch) fallback | local facts projected through the SAME function before comparing against the wire values: `head` = `ref_identity(commit) == pr.head_sha`, `branch` = `ref_identity(branch) == pr.head_branch` |

The raw values stay raw **only** in the comparison input (`GitFacts` cache) and in the
wire sanitizer's input; no egress carries them (`apply_pr_facts` and `reapply_path` read
the same cache and project at each use).

Out-of-fence consumers that receive the projection WITHOUT any file outside the fence
changing (disclosed, not participants — they copy the attribution fact for display and
never compare it): `src/adapters/herdr.rs:1775-1800` (fresh record) and
`src/adapters/herdr.rs:1964-1967` (`preserve_workspace`). That is why `record_branch`
(`integrate/mod.rs:245/271`) stores the projected value: it is the single choke point on
the attribution, so the Herdr egress is closed by construction.

## WIRE CHANGE

**No schema change.** Field names, types, presence and the GraphQL query are untouched;
only the VALUE of a credential-shaped ref changes, on both the gh wire and the local
workspace fields:

- before (pre-fix): `headRefName: "test-ghp_FAKE563…"` passed through raw; local
  `workspace.branch: "test-ghp_FAKE563…"` reached `/snapshot` + SSE raw.
- after: `headRefName: "[REDACTED:sha256:2bf52de77c710de3c622965953d93edb455b29d1ed2eec468b2d3089ce2a1c68]"`.
- ordinary refs (`ws2/gh-plane`, `abc123`, real 40-hex oids) are byte-unchanged.

Consumers: the daemon's gh plane state (PR matching, `pr_number`/`ci_status`/`issues` —
`GhRepoState` is never serialized to clients) and `workspace.branch`/`workspace.head_sha`
in `/snapshot` + SSE deltas (display-only fields on the iOS client; they are opaque
strings, so no client/schema version change). Values remain deterministic across polls,
restarts and daemon generations (SHA-256 of the raw value, no salt and no clock).

## #563 two-sided hashed-ref identity merge entry (2026-09-17)

Branch `g563-hashedid`, reviewed head `99d953447dc11eed0df9132c6b867474c9c723f3`, merged into `integration`.
Owner-approved **option A**: a credential-shaped git ref must never egress to the phone, and redaction must not cost
a PR binding.

**The fix.** The gh plane folds `headRefOid`/`headRefName` to a hashed identity at the response boundary
(`src/adapters/gh_plane.rs:844-862`; placeholder `[REDACTED:sha256:<hex-of-raw>]` built at `:825-830` via
`core::provenance::text_sha256_hex`, passthrough helper `ref_identity` at `:812-818`), replacing the old raw-preserve
carve-out. The local side compares the SAME hash (`src/integrate/mod.rs`), so raw-equality binding becomes
hash-equality on both sides and BOTH bindings keep working: the head-SHA path and the committed-but-unpushed branch
fallback. The entropy rule now applies to the ref fields and to the raw LOCAL branch copy
(`integrate/mod.rs:384-390`) with identity preserved by the same hash.

**Third participant, found by the lane itself, inside the fence:** `src/integrate/mod.rs:355` (the `converge`
attribution fallback) was a third place the local branch reached the read model and re-introduced the RAW value; fixed
at the recording boundary (`:245`/`:271`), which also closes the un-fenced Herdr egress by construction.

**Evidence (raw exits).** red-base: `--test gh_plane g563_` 101 (2 failed, RAW value on the left) and
`--test integration credential_shaped` 101 (1 failed). green-fixed: 0 (2 passed) and 0 (1 passed).
**red-mutation-naive-mask: 101 (2 failed)** - naive masking is shown to BREAK the binding, which is the failure mode
option A exists to prevent - then a byte-identical restore and green. Lib legs: `cargo test --lib g563_` 0
(`corrald` block 1 passed / 327 filtered out; `corrald_client` block 0 passed / 14 filtered out).

**Independent review: PASS, no blocking findings.** It re-derived the RED at the base in its own probe worktree (101,
with the exact failure shape), spliced the naive-mask mutation itself (101, `branch-leg never bound PR 563: observed
pr_number=None pr_match_source=None branch=Some("[REDACTED:sha256:2bf5...]")` - the binding genuinely lost), and
verified the two-sided comparison from the CODE rather than the comment. It also verified the one leg that does NOT
discriminate the mutation and found the root cause in code: `tests/integration.rs:977-987` constructs the wire state
itself with its own placeholder values, so `redact_response` is never in that path - disclosed by the lane, coverage
union intact (the gh_plane e2e does run the real sanitizer + integrator).

**Orchestrator battery at the same head:** focused-red 0 (2 passed), focused-int 0 (1 passed), `cargo test
--workspace` 0, fmt 0, clippy 0, deny 0, audit 0; head unchanged; clean tree.

**RETRACTION (orchestrator, recorded because it was wrong and it reached a reviewer's brief):** I initially claimed
two "green" lib legs were vacuous (`0 passed; 14 filtered out`) and that the report's "1 passed" contradicted its log.
That was MY error: `cargo test --lib <filter>` prints a result block per binary, and I quoted the `corrald_client`
block. The `corrald` block reads 1 passed / 327 filtered out in both logs and the tests exist
(`src/adapters/gh_plane.rs:1771`, `:1746`). Withdrawn in the review brief (item C rewritten) and in `.amend-1.md`
before the reviewer spawned; the reviewer independently refuted it three ways. Lesson recorded: attribute every
`test result:` line to its own binary before concluding a leg is vacuous.

**Non-blocking findings for follow-up:** (1) `tests/integration.rs:977-987` deserves an in-code comment stating its
wire state is pre-redacted by construction, so the next reader does not over-trust it; (2) `src/core/redact.rs:47-50`
carries a stale doc sentence that now contradicts shipped behaviour (docs-only, correctly left out of the fence).

is stated once here and is what a future container in this sheet must reuse; the only remaining
container surface in the sheet is the block-card stack, which already obeys it.

All four panels: **backing REMOVED**, copy on the translucent sheet surface, `.padding(.horizontal, 16)`

## CONTRAST (method + measured numbers, Day and Night, per state)

**Method.** Two axes, both with the repo's own WCAG math (`SheetBackdrop`, `AppTheme.swift:765-855`):

1. **Measured, from the produced frames** (`SheetPolishTests.testRenderedSheetStatesDayNightNormalAndLargeText`):
   the 3x render is scanned in the sheet's presented region; the copy's ink pixels are located by exact
   colour, the backdrop is the **median of the non-ink pixels on the ink's own rows** (the actual
   rendered backdrop behind the copy), and the ratio is `SheetBackdrop.contrastRatio(ink, backdrop)`.
   Numbers are printed as `G569_CONTRAST ...` lines in `docs/evidence/issue-569/measurements.log` and
   asserted `>= 4.5` in-test.
2. **Worst-case model, the #416 lock** (`testStateCopyTierHoldsAAOverTheWorstCaseTintedBackdrop`):
   `SheetBackdrop.worstContrast(ink:tint: base@0.8, over: every palette token)` — the bound that covers
   the real translucent backdrop where the board shows through (the `presentationBackground` material is
   not composited by the XCTest renderer, which is why axis 1 alone would understate the constraint).

**Measured (axis 1).** Day (Latte) and Night (Mocha), every state, normal + AX3:

| state | Day ink → backdrop | ratio | Night ink → backdrop | ratio |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| loading | `#4c4f69` → `#ffffff` | **7.99** | `#cdd6f4` → `#1c1c1e` | **11.77** |
| empty | same | **7.99** | same | **11.77** |
| error | same | **7.99** | same | **11.77** |
| permission | same | **7.99** (AX3: `#eff1f5` → **7.06**) | same | **11.77** |
| error glyph (non-text, needs 3:1) | `#d20f39` → `#ffffff` | **5.43** | `#f38ba8` → `#1c1c1e` | **7.35** |

**Worst case (axis 2), the number that binds the real translucent backdrop.** The AA tier the copy now
rides: `#4c4f69` / `#cdd6f4` over `base@0.8` tinted over ANY of the 26 palette tokens → **5.04:1 Latte /
6.44:1 Mocha** (>= 4.5, asserted). The tiers that were NOT usable without a backing (printed by the same
test, per flavor): `tailMuted` **3.95 / 4.19**, `tailQuiet` **2.50 / 1.91**, red-as-text **3.43 / 4.02**,
mauve **3.42 / 4.59**. That is exactly the AA debt the #428 opaque backing was paying for the state copy;
with the copy on the AA tier the debt is gone and **no state needed a backing**. (The red channel was not
flattened — it rides the warning glyph, which is non-text and clears 3:1 by 5.43 / 7.35.)

**No state failed, so no state kept a backing.** The one alternative a reviewer may prefer: red text +
a per-state rounded card for the generic error. It is not needed for AA; the numbers above are what a
decision either way should be made against.

## WHAT I DID NOT VERIFY

- **The real translucent backdrop's absolute pixels**: the XCTest renderer does not composite
  `presentationBackground`, so the frames' backdrop is the system sheet fill (white / `#1c1c1e`). The
  app-surface guarantee therefore rests on the repo's locked worst-case model (5.04 / 6.44 for the copy
  tier), which is the same model #385/#416 used to lock the tint — not on an on-device pixel sample.
- **Physical-device look/feel** — the owner's gate; not claimed here.
- **The `.empty` next-step copy and the red→glyph decision** are judgement calls inside the brief; the
  CONTRAST section gives the numbers for the alternative (red text + a per-state card) if the owner or
  reviewer prefers it.
- **The full-suite skip** (1 test) is the pre-existing `G551_MEASURE`-gated
  `ForegroundReconnectTests.testWarmReturnStageMeasurements`, not a lane test.
- **Hosted CI** at this head: none (no push by this lane; the orchestrator owns promotion).

## Round-2 receipt — the regression claim is REFUTED; no change; the experiment is reverted (orchestrator, 2026-09-17)

`.amend-2.md` claimed that head `60c35e96` deterministically fails the protected check
`RecentWorktreeBlockTests.testRenderedSheetDayNightMediumLargeAndAX3` while the base passes.
`.amend-2-retraction.md` retracts that: the orchestrator's own controlled A/B (three isolated runs at this
head, three at the base, back-to-back on one host) has the head passing 3/3 with rc=0 and a clean
`abcdef1`, and the reviewer's full-suite run at this head passed the test 8/8 while establishing that the
code drawing that row is outside this lane's diff (label builder `:5767-5836`, block renderer
`:5840-5905`, backdrop mount at `:6014` — which falls between the lane's hunks `5957 < 6014 < 6047`).
The assertion is an OCR readback of an anti-aliased monospaced sha blended with the demo board text; it
merges characters (`abodef1` for `abcdef1`) at some render states, and that fragility exists at the base
as well.

**What this round did.** I reproduced one failure on an iPhone 17 Pro simulator (device class
`iPhone18,1`, the class `hermes-sim-task` ran) and, on that single sample, applied an experimental
`RepoLabelChip.compact` variant: the capsule drawn exactly as the Board draws it (2 pt padding, dot, hue
ink, border — measured 19.0 pt), with its layout box shrunk by that padding so the caption row keeps its
pre-#569 height and the #558 worktree block sits where #558 measured it. The claim was refuted before the
round closed, so the experiment is **reverted in full** (revert commit `bc720ce`, which unwinds
`9923465` + `cf67e5e`): source, release-digest re-pin, source pin and evidence refresh all return to the
round-1 delivery. `git diff 60c35e96 HEAD` is empty and `FleetViews.swift`'s blob is again
`1890a0f2cd2e7f737cd7ac3641bc01e7ee2dc4cda98821a107ae8831cac1a295`. The #558 test and its fixture were
never touched; the decided look, the 16/10 grid, the chips, the corner convention and the commit row's
typography are unchanged; nothing is pushed.

**Standing verification at the reverted head (`bc720ce`)** — raw exits, load = this host's 1-minute
average at each run's start (other fleet lanes were running throughout):

| run | device | raw exit | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| isolated #558 check | iPhone 17 Pro | `rc=65` | load 149.44 — assertion `FleetNotifierTests.swift:143`, OCR `abodef1` |
| isolated #558 check | iPhone 17 Pro | `rc=65` | load 93.83 — same assertion |
| isolated #558 check | iPhone 17 Pro | `rc=65` | load 107.87 — same assertion |
| isolated #558 check | iPhone 16 | `rc=0` | load 139.92 — 16 x `abcdef1`, no assertion |
| isolated #558 check | iPhone 17 Pro | `rc=65` | load 22.10 (after the fleet load dropped) — same assertion |
| full `FleetNotifierTests` | iPhone 17 Pro | `rc=65` | load 149.56 — `Executed 669 tests, with 1 test skipped and 1 failure`; the failure IS this #558 check |
| full `FleetNotifierTests` | iPhone 16 | `rc=65` | load 103.60 — `Executed 669 tests, with 1 test skipped and 1 failure`; the failure is `EnrollmentPairingFlowTests.testEqualRawAgentIdsOnDifferentHostsCannotShareCredentialsOrReadRoutes` (`EnrollmentPairingFlowTests.swift:633`, NSURLErrorDomain -1004 under the same load) — the #558 check PASSED in this suite |
| base `23bbfa66`, isolated | iPhone 17 Pro | `rc=0` | load 222.57 — 16 x `abcdef1`, run minutes after the failures above |
| release gate `--self-test` / pin check | — | `RELEASE_SELFTEST_EXIT=0` / `PINCHECK_EXIT=0` | |

So on this host today the check is **not attributable to the lane and not stable**: at the standing
(delivery) head it failed five consecutive isolated runs on the iPhone 17 Pro (loads 22–186), while the
same head passed 8/8 frames on that same simulator minutes earlier at the intermediate head, the same
head passes on the iPhone 16, and the base passes. That is the environment-fragility the retraction
describes, and the failures are reported here raw rather than smoothed over.

**Note, not a request:** the intermediate variant (`9923465`) is what passed 8/8 on this simulator right
before the revert (`git revert bc720ce` would restore it). It changes nothing about the decided look —
only that the chip stops sizing the caption row, putting the #558 block back at the base y. If this
check's margin on this host matters for the fleet's merge gate, that commit is the ready-made option;
this lane stands on the delivered bytes as instructed. Logs: `/tmp/g569-stand.log`,
`/tmp/g569-stand-low.log`, `/tmp/g569-base-now.log`, `/tmp/g569-fix-gates.log`, `/tmp/g569-final2.log`.

## Round-3 receipt — the regression is REAL; the retraction was wrong; the fix is re-applied (orchestrator, 2026-09-18)

`.amend-3-reapply.md` corrects `.amend-2-retraction.md`: the orchestrator's first A/B ran at 23:17:46, after
this lane committed the fix `9923465` at 23:15:50, so its "head" arm was already standing on the FIXED tree
(it passed — evidence the fix works) and was misread as "the delivered head passes". Their corrected A/B,
alternating with each arm pinned to an exact commit across host loads 16–227, shows the delivered
(post-revert) code failing 6/6 (`rc=65`) and base `23bbfa66` passing 6/6 (`rc=0`). The diagnosis in
`9923465` — the chip must keep the Board's capsule drawing without inheriting the Board's row height — was
right.

**The fix is re-applied** in `d10f0b2` (reversing `bc720ce`). `FleetViews.swift` is back to the `9923465`
bytes (`ef21a8585f8cedf33fbdb2f5822d6fcb4b822bc16b852c6e7e54878167875535`), the evidence refresh is
restored, the owner's decided look / grid / chips / corner convention are unchanged, the #558 test and
fixture were never touched, and nothing is pushed.
